Displaced Palestinians gather to receive food from a charity in [Deir al-Balah](1470463.md), Gaza Strip (June 2024).

The population of the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) underwent a [famine](51329.md) as a result of an [Israeli blockade](75013719.md) during the [Gaza war](74998519.md) between 22 August 2025 and 19 December 2025.[^cite_note-5][^cite_note-reuters20251219-6] The prevention of basic essentials and [humanitarian aid](76066918.md) from entering Gaza,[^cite_note-7][^cite_note-8][^cite_note-un20240318-9] as well as [airstrikes](76139149.md) that destroyed food infrastructure, such as bakeries, mills, and food stores, caused a widespread scarcity of essential supplies.[^cite_note-11] Humanitarian aid was also blocked by [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_blockade_of_aid_delivery_to_the_Gaza_Strip) at borders and ports. Increasing [societal breakdown in Gaza](77392232.md), including looting, was also cited as a barrier to the provision of aid.

By August 2025, [Integrated Food Security Phase Classification](16431152.md) (IPC) projections showed that 100% of the population are experiencing "high levels of acute food insecurity", and 32% were projected to face Phase 5 catastrophic levels by 30 September 2025.[^cite_note-IPC_22_August_2025-4] On 22 August 2025, the IPC said that famine is taking place in at least one of the five [governorates](5811262.md) in the Gaza Strip: specifically, the [Gaza Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Governorate) which includes [Gaza City](55387.md). The IPC added that, within the next month, famine was likely to also occur in both the [Deir al-Balah Governorate](14704596.md) and the [Khan Yunis Governorate](13900254.md). The IPC had insufficient data on the [North Gaza Governorate](8689663.md) for a classification but concluded that conditions were likely similar or worse than in the Gaza Governorate.[^cite_note-IPC_22_August_2025-4][^cite_note-reuters20250822-12] Within the next 6 weeks as of 16 August, the number of people in IPC Phase 5 was expected to rise from 500,000 to over 640,000.[^cite_note-13]

On 22 October 2025, the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) found that Israel is obliged to guarantee sufficient food to Gazans and to allow [UNRWA](32270.md) to operate in Gaza.[^cite_note-ICJ_2025Oct22_obligations_IL-14] On 19 December, the IPC published a report declaring that there is no longer a famine in Gaza after improvements in food delivery following the ceasefire, but warned that the situation remains "highly fragile" with 100,000 still facing "catastrophic conditions", although that number was projected to decline to 1,900 by April 2026.[^cite_note-reuters20251219-6][^cite_note-apnews20251219-15] As of January 2026, Gazans have received enough humanitarian aid to meet their basic food needs for the first time since the start of the war.[^cite_note-16]

However, in late July 2026, the IPC reported that the scale-up of humanitarian assistance, enabled by the October 2025 ceasefire, has driven improvements in food security and nutrition conditions across Gaza, but that over 1.2 million people (59% of the population of Gaza) continued to face Phase 3 Crisis levels of food insecurity or worse, including 212,000 (about 10% of Gazans) experiencing Phase 4 Emergency levels, and warned that Gaza is still experiencing a severe food emergency that could develop into another famine.[^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18]

## Before the Gaza war



Map of the Gaza Strip with Israeli/Egyptian-controlled borders and limited fishing zone as of April 2019.

Further information: [Blockade of the Gaza Strip](19224310.md)

Israel imposed restrictions on movement and goods in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) since the early 1990s.[^cite_note-19] After [Hamas](13913.md) took over in 2007, Israel significantly intensified existing movement restrictions and imposed a complete [blockade](293054.md) on the movement of goods and people in and out of the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-20]

[Rhoda Howard-Hassmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhoda_Howard-Hassmann) argued in 2016 that "thoroughly planned impoverishment" has been a long-term policy of Israel for the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward-Hassmann2016126-21] According to [Sara Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Roy), a leading expert on the Gazan economy,

> The current desecration of Gaza is the latest stage in a process that has taken increasingly violent forms over time. In the fifty-six years since it [occupied](76465951.md) the Strip in 1967, Israel has transformed Gaza from a territory politically and economically integrated with Israel and the West Bank into an isolated enclave, from a functional economy to a dysfunctional one, from a productive society to an impoverished one. It has likewise removed Gaza's residents from the sphere of politics, transforming them from a people with a nationalist claim to a population whose majority requires some form of humanitarian aid to sustain themselves.[^cite_note-22]

In the early 2000s, the Gaza Strip witnessed a period of increasing tensions that had a profound impact on its economic and agricultural sectors.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward-Hassmann2016126-21] During this time, a relatively small number of Israeli settlers lived in the Gaza Strip, yet they controlled a significant portion of the territory's valuable resources. Settlers had access to about 25% of the Strip, an area that included 40% of Gaza's arable land, as well as a substantial share of its water resources, limiting the availability of land and water for the Palestinian population.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward-Hassmann2016125-23] After [Israel withdrew its settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_Gaza) from the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) in 2005, [Palestinian elections](38945340.md) were held in 2006, which Hamas won. In response to the election results, Israel designated the governing party and the Gaza Strip as a "hostile entity", implementing a blockade along with economic sanctions and restrictions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith201939-24] As a part of the blockade Israel imposed restrictions on imports of cooking fuel and gas into Gaza. Lists of restrictions were generated based on what Israel defines as 'dual use' goods, items that might be put to military uses threatening Israel's security.[^cite_note-25] These measures are contested internationally and criticized for exacerbating economic and living conditions in Gaza.[^cite_note-26]

Prior to the blockade, Gaza's population stood at 1.6 million, serviced by 400 trucks carrying goods into the Strip every day. Under the new policy, according to the Israeli [NGO](46539.md) [Gisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisha_\(human_rights_organization\)), Israel proposed to permit only 106 trucks entry to deliver goods.[^cite_note-Reuters2012-27] In the following decades, the number of humanitarian trucks permitted to enter Gaza varied, depending on several factors such as the political situation, security issues, agreements between Israel and Palestinian authorities, and interventions by international organizations. To obtain permission to import any commodity into the Strip, proof had to be supplied that they were indispensable, often causing delays and complications in the supply of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFiliu201457-28] Under Netanyahu's second premiership, Israel sporadically allowed more humanitarian aid, but tightened it during conflicts with Hamas and [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md).[^cite_note-Slater2021-29] The blockade was maintained to secure *de facto* control over Gaza, along with limited economic assistance and humanitarian aid to prevent Israeli reoccupation.[^cite_note-Slater2021-29]

Precise calculations were made to determine the minimum calorific requirement to avoid malnutrition in the Gaza Strip, and these formed the basis for Israel's determination of the truck numbers for food supplies from 2007 to 2010.[^cite_note-Reuters2012-27][^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31][^cite_note-32] [Dov Weissglas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dov_Weissglas), an advisor to the then Israeli PM [Ehud Olmert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Olmert), explained: "We have to make them much thinner, but not enough to die,"[^cite_note-Strand2014-33] the idea being "to put the Palestinians on a diet, but not to make them die of hunger."[^cite_note-34][^cite_note-35] The calculation excluded factors such as the collapse of agriculture due to the blockade which dried up access to seed markets.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward-Hassmann2016127-36][^cite_note-37]

Diplomatic cables subsequently published by [WikiLeaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WikiLeaks) in 2011 revealed that Israel had informed the United States in 2008 that, while it would take measures to prevent a humanitarian crisis, it intended to keep Gaza's economy on the "brink of collapse".[^cite_note-38] Gazans faced extreme poverty due to the blockade;[^cite_note-Slater2021-29] in 2020, according to a report by Steven Devereux of the [Institute of Development Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Development_Studies) (IDS), "more than half the residents of Gaza survived below the poverty line, two-thirds were food insecure, and three-quarters received international aid, either in cash or as food."[^cite_note-39]

The [Goldstone Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goldstone_Report) discovered that during the [2008–2009 Gaza War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_War_\(2008%E2%80%932009\)), Israel's invasion had caused deliberate and massive destruction of Gaza's agricultural sector.[^cite_note-41] Israel also declared 30% of the most arable land in the Strip [no-go zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No-go_areas). After 2012, the [Red Cross](15487.md) secured an agreement to allow Gazan farmers to cultivate crops of various heights, in areas respectively at 300 metres to 1 kilometre from Israel's [fortified border fence](478337.md). Both cultivators and their rudimentary irrigation devices nonetheless were often exposed to sniping and automated machinegun fire, and crops along the armistice line were, without warning, sprayed with [Monsanto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsanto)'s [Roundup herbicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roundup_\(herbicide\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith201939–40-42] Likewise, Israel placed severe restrictions on fishing within Gaza's waters—the 20 nautical miles agreed to under the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords) were unilaterally reduced to nine—with fishable areas demarcated with buoys.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFiliu201456-43] In 2009, Israel further reduced this to a 3-nautical-mile limit with the result that 85% of Gaza's fishing water was blocked by Israeli warships.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward-Hassmann2016125–127-44] Israeli gunships reportedly fired on local fishermen even within these areas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith201940-45]

In August 2023, it was reported that 12,076 truckloads of authorized goods entered Gaza, reflecting a slight improvement in the volume of goods allowed into the territory compared to previous periods. Despite these inflows, the volume of goods remained insufficient, given the 60% increase in population since 2007 and the escalating needs of the Gazan population.[^cite_note-46]

The humanitarian impact of the blockade is compounded by recurrent hostilities, which not only result in high casualty rates but also further degrade Gaza's already fragile infrastructure. The United Nations and various human rights organizations have repeatedly called for the lifting of the blockade and for increased humanitarian access to alleviate the suffering of Gaza's residents. The situation in 2023, where the region has witnessed the highest number of fatalities since 2005, underscores the urgent need for a comprehensive and lasting solution to the humanitarian crisis in Gaza.[^cite_note-47]

## Start of crisis

Further information: [Israeli blockade of the Gaza Strip (2023–present)](75013719.md) and [October 7 attacks](74998317.md)



Four-year-old Palestinian girl who died from malnutrition and lack of treatment (August 2024).

| External videos |
| --- |
| video icon [Emily "Cali" Callahan, an American nurse who worked in Gaza for Doctors Without Borders until early November, describes the humanitarian situation in Gaza to CNN's Anderson Cooper](https://edition.cnn.com/videos/world/2023/11/08/american-nurse-gaza-emily-cali-callahan-intv-ac360-vpx.cnn)[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49] |

Following the [7 October attack](74998317.md), Israel announced on 9 October 2023 that it was blocking the entry of food and water into Gaza.[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-bbc-51][^cite_note-cnn20231007-52] Because Gaza was already mostly reliant on food aid, the repercussions were felt immediately. On 18 October, Alia Zaki, a spokesperson for the WFP, stated that the population of Gaza was at risk of starvation.[^cite_note-Struggle_to_Save-53] Three days later, the UN released a statement saying food stocks were nearly exhausted.[^cite_note-UN_update_on_humanitarian_needs-54] By 23 October, [Cindy McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cindy_McCain), executive director of the UN WFP, stated people were "literally starving to death as we speak".[^cite_note-55]

On 27 October, a spokesperson for the WFP stated food and other basic supplies were running out.[^cite_note-56] On 3 November, UN officials stated the average Gazan diet consisted of only two pieces of bread per day,[^cite_note-Lederer-57] and [ActionAid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ActionAid) stated more than half a million Gazans faced death by starvation.[^cite_note-cbsnews20231108-58] On 11 November, Corinne Fleischer, Middle East regional director of the WFP, stated, "hundreds of people are queueing for hours every day to get bread rations at bakeries," as people were being pushed "closer to starvation."[^cite_note-59]

## Damage

### Damage to infrastructure

See also: [Israeli bombing of the Gaza Strip](76139149.md)



Baking bread during Gaza war 23–25

In 2023 and 2024, Israeli airstrikes severely impacted Gaza's food supply, targeting bakeries, agricultural land, and food infrastructure. The damage led to widespread closures and shortages, affecting access to bread, flour, and other food staples. [Michael Fakhri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Fakhri), the U.N. Special Rapporteur on the Right to Food, has stated such actions amount to an Israeli "starvation campaign" waged by destroying Gaza's food system.[^cite_note-60]

In October 2023, Israeli airstrikes targeted bakeries and agricultural sites in Gaza. On 18 October, an airstrike destroyed a bakery in the [Nuseirat Camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuseirat_Camp), killing four bakers.[^cite_note-Struggle_to_Save-53][^cite_note-61] Subsequent strikes hit other bakeries, and by 24 October, many had closed, with those still open facing long lines.[^cite_note-Palestinians_trapped_in_Gaza-62][^cite_note-63] By 28 October, a fifth of Gaza's bakeries were destroyed.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65] In early November, Israel bombed one of Gaza City's last bakeries, and [UNOCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOCHA) reported over half of Gaza's bakeries had been destroyed.[^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67] By mid-November, no bakeries were operating in northern Gaza, and the last flour mill was bombed.[^cite_note-cbsnews20231108-58][^cite_note-68][^cite_note-hrw20231218-69] Israeli strikes also damaged fishing ports, warehouses, and farmland, with 22% of farmland destroyed by 12 December.[^cite_note-Israel_is_starving-10][^cite_note-70]

In 2024, [UNOSAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOSAT) reported 33% of agricultural land was damaged by February.[^cite_note-71] In June, an Israeli missile hit one of [Deir al-Balah](1470463.md)'s two flour mills, also damaging a WFP warehouse.[^cite_note-72] By July, bakeries could not open due to fuel shortages.[^cite_note-73] In October, UNOSAT and the [Food and Agriculture Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_and_Agriculture_Organization) estimated 67.6% of Gaza's croplands had been damaged, severely impacting Gaza's food production.[^cite_note-74]

&#x20;There has been extensive environmental damage caused by the ongoing [Israeli invasion of the Gaza Strip](74998519.md) (itself a part of the [Gaza war](74998519.md)),

[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_forensic-architecture-20240329-75]

&#x20;including the destruction of agricultural land, displacement of people, [bombing of Gaza](76139149.md), the [Israeli blockade](75013719.md), and famine in the Gaza Strip.

[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_forensic-architecture-20240329-75]

[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_theguardian20240329-76]

[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_theguardian20240415-77]

&#x20;By March 2024, nearly half of the farmland in Gaza had been destroyed,

[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_forensic-architecture-20240329-75]

[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_theguardian20240329-76]

&#x20;and by the following January 80% of the tree cover had been destroyed.

[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_yale20250206-78]



IDF bulldozer clearing trees in Gaza in October 2023

Israeli [bombardment](76139149.md) and the [blockade](75013719.md) have led to a total collapse of Gaza's civil infrastructure, including sewage treatment, waste disposal, water management, and fuel supplies. Water has been polluted by 130,000 cubic metres of sewage being discharged into the sea every day due to Israel cutting off fuel supplies. Groundwater has been contaminated by toxins and munitions and air has been polluted by smoke and particulates from bombing.

[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_forensic-architecture-20240329-75]

[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_Saber-79]

&#x20;Soils have been degraded by uprooting trees and contaminated by toxins, munitions, heavy bombing and demolitions. Bombing by the Israeli army has created 50 million tonnes of debris and hazardous material, much of which contains human remains and tens of thousands of bombs. In June 2024, northern Gaza was described as a "wasteland", unable to sustain life.

[^cite_note-80]

The size and lasting impact of the systematic and intentional destruction of [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Palestine) in Gaza have led to calls by the research group [Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture) at [Goldsmiths, University of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goldsmiths,_University_of_London), and the [Palestinian Environmental NGOs Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Environmental_NGOs_Network) for the Israeli Government to be investigated for the [Rome Statute](68762.md) war crime of [ecocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecocide) for "widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment".[^cite_note-Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war_theguardian20240329-76]

## Emergency and threat of famine

### Timeline

#### November–December 2023

Cindy McCain stated on 17 November that civilians faced the immediate possibility of starvation.[^cite_note-81] Ten days later, McCain stated Gaza was on the brink of [famine](51329.md),[^cite_note-82] as begging for food became the "new norm."[^cite_note-83] On 1 December, an [Integrated Food Security Phase Classification](16431152.md) report, based on a committee of independent experts, placed almost the entire population of Gaza (93% or 2.08 million) at IPC Phase 3 or above (Crisis or worse) with 79% in Emergency (IPC Phase 4),[^cite_note-84] and 15% (378,000 people) in Catastrophe (IPC Phase 5).[^cite_note-B'tselem-85]

On 7 December, the WFP stated 97% of households had inadequate food consumption and 83% in southern Gaza were surviving through "extreme consumption strategies."[^cite_note-86] By 10 December, the UN, international aid organizations, and relief workers in Gaza warned of mass starvation.[^cite_note-87] A representative for [Medical Aid for Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_Aid_for_Palestinians) stated, "The hunger wars have started."[^cite_note-88] On 15 December, the United Nations estimated nine out of ten residents were not eating food every day.[^cite_note-89]

On 20 December, the United Nations stated people in Gaza were experiencing "alarming levels of hunger never before witnessed in Gaza".[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91] On 21 December, the United Nations stated more than half a million people in the Gaza Strip were [starving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starving).[^cite_note-92] On 22 December, [UNICEF](19698110.md) warned of the increasingly growing threat of famine in the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-93] On 29 December, [Mercy Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercy_Corps) stated half a million people faced "catastrophic hunger and starvation".[^cite_note-94]

| 1 Apr – 10 May 2025[^cite_note-99] | 244,000 | 12% | 925,000 | 44% | 775,000 | 37% | 156,000 | 7% |

#### January 2024

By 1 January 2024 90% of Palestinians in Gaza regularly went without food.[^cite_note-100] The IDF alleged Hamas stole humanitarian aid;[^cite_note-101] killed people seeking humanitarian aid;[^cite_note-102] and keeps its own supply reserves.[^cite_note-TI-103] The US and the UN both denied Israeli claims that Hamas looting plays a significant role in the lack of aid, with a senior US official stating that "the Israeli government has not brought to the attention of the US government... any specific evidence of Hamas theft or diversion of assistance provided via the U.N. and its agencies. Full stop."[^cite_note-DeYoung_Hudson_2024_g056-104]

On 3 January 2024, Arif Husain, the chief economist at the WFP, stated 80% of all people in the world experiencing famine or catastrophic hunger were in the Gaza Strip, stating, "In my life, I've never seen anything like this in terms of severity".[^cite_note-105] Food prices rose in Gaza as food stocks were "running low".[^cite_note-106]

The United Nations humanitarian chief [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths) stated on 5 January 2024, "People are facing the highest levels of food insecurity ever recorded."[^cite_note-107] [Alex de Waal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_de_Waal), an expert on humanitarian crises and international law, stated, "The rigor, scale and speed of the destruction of the structures necessary for survival, and enforcement of the siege, surpasses any other case of man-made famine in the last 75 years."[^cite_note-Looming_Starvation_in_Gaza-108] [António Guterres](471806.md) stated, "The long shadow of starvation is stalking the people of Gaza".[^cite_note-109]

On 16 January 2024, UNOCHA reported 378,000 people in Gaza were in IPC Phase 5, or catastrophic levels of hunger.[^cite_note-110][^cite_note-ohchr20240116-111] It reported all 2.2 million people in the Gaza Strip were facing acute food insecurity – the highest proportion of a population experiencing starvation in recorded history.[^cite_note-112] The Famine Review Committee (FRC) which compiled the Gaza data on famine in terms of the Integrated Food Security Phase Classification (IPC) on 1 December 2023, forecast that the total population will be at Phase 3 by 7 February and that 25% or 500,000 Gazans will reach Phase 5.[^cite_note-B'tselem-85]

On 21 January 2024, a journalist in the Gaza Strip reported that people were making flour using animal food.[^cite_note-113] On 21 January 2024, the UN reported there were only 15 bakeries still in operation across the entirety of the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-114] By 30 January 2024, *[CNN](62028.md)* reported that Palestinians were eating grass to stay alive.[^cite_note-cnn20240130-115] On 31 January, the World Health Organization's emergencies director stated, "This is a population that is starving to death."[^cite_note-116]

#### February–April 2024

On 12 February, the [Food and Agriculture Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_and_Agriculture_Organization) stated there were "unprecedented levels of acute food insecurity, hunger, and near-famine-like conditions in Gaza."[^cite_note-117] Israel attacked fishermen in [Deir el-Balah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_el-Balah) attempting to catch fish to eat.[^cite_note-118] On 27 February 2024, Carl Skau, the deputy executive director of the WFP, told the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) that more than 500,000 people were at risk of imminent famine in Gaza.[^cite_note-famine-119]

On 17 February 2024, ActionAid stated that "every single person in the territory" was facing extreme levels of hunger, stating that people had even run out of animal feed to eat.[^cite_note-120] By March, bird feed in northern Gaza had run out and people ate livestock feed.[^cite_note-121] On 3 March, the Gaza government media office stated, "the famine is still deepening".[^cite_note-122] In March 2024, experts, such as the [United Nations Special Rapporteur on the Right to Food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Special_Rapporteur_on_the_Right_to_Food), warned that Gaza might already be experiencing famine; while Jeremy Konyndyk, the president of [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International), stated that "large-scale famine mortality" would soon begin.[^cite_note-123] On 7 March, UNOCHA stated that the annual rate of [food inflation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_inflation) was 118% in January 2024, while the [consumer price index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_price_index) for food rose nearly 105%.[^cite_note-124] March [Epidemiological](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epidemiology) forecasts projected future deaths in Gaza from all causes, including epidemics, as ranging from 48,210 to 193,180 by August 2024.[^cite_note-We_are_about_to_witness-125]

On 13 March 2024, Israeli spokesperson [Daniel Hagari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Hagari) said they were "trying to flood the area" with aid and that they were "learning and improving and doing different changes."[^cite_note-126] In late March, António Guterres stated that it was time to "truly flood Gaza with lifesaving aid" and that the starvation was a "moral outrage"[^cite_note-127] Israeli Ministry of Strategic Affairs [Ron Dermer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Dermer) said that reports of an imminent famine were "a complete lie and fabrication" and "a libel against Israel."[^cite_note-128] In April, Yoav Gallant announced that Israel would create a new crossing on its border and that "we plan to flood Gaza with aid".[^cite_note-129] [Philippe Lazzarini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_Lazzarini) stated on 30 April that "there is more food available on the market", but "no cash circulating" in northern Gaza, making the food inaccessible. He further acknowledged the increase in supplies entering Gaza in April, but cautioned that it was "still far from enough to reverse the negative trend".[^cite_note-130]

In early April 2024, the Red Cross said that 2.2 million people in Gaza were considered to be experiencing food insecurity at the emergency level.[^cite_note-131]

On 30 April 2024, when rendering its verdict in the *[Nicaragua v. Germany](76228006.md)* genocide case, the International Court of Justice said that it remained concerned about the situation in Gaza, "In particular, in view of the prolonged and widespread deprivation of food and other basic necessities to which they have been subjected".[^cite_note-132] In its March 2024 interim ruling, the ICJ stated, "The court observes that Palestinians in Gaza are no longer facing only a risk of famine ... but that famine is setting in."[^cite_note-reuters20240328-133]

Following the Israeli military’s drone attack on three clearly marked World Central Kitchen (WCK) trucks in April 2024, Israel’s chief military prosecutor announced in August 2026 that no criminal proceedings would be initiated regarding the incident. The trucks were carrying food aid for the people of Gaza. According to a report by The Guardian and interviews with senior Israeli officers, endangering civilians and aid workers is not a consideration when Israeli forces undertake civil control missions in Gaza.[^cite_note-134]

#### May–July 2024

In May 2024, the head of humanitarian policy and advocacy at Save the Children, stated the official count of 28 children dead from malnutrition and disease was "probably the tip of the iceberg", due to the [destruction of Gaza's healthcare system](75314000.md).[^cite_note-135]

In May 2024, the WFP reported, "The threat of famine in Gaza never loomed larger".[^cite_note-136] The [United Nations humanitarian chief](1607007.md) stated famine in Gaza was an "immediate, clear and present danger" as food ran out and humanitarian operations were stuck.[^cite_note-137] The Gaza media office said 98% of bakeries were closed.[^cite_note-138] In June 2024, the WFP and the Food and Agriculture Organization stated in a joint statement that more than 1 million people could face severe starvation within a month.[^cite_note-139] The same month, the World Health Organization stated only two nutrition stabilisation centers remained operational in the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-140]

The [Famine Early Warning Systems Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Famine_Early_Warning_Systems_Network) (FEWS NET) reported later in May that famine was possible in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDevereux202410-141] The FRC, citing uncertainty and the disparate evidence used in the FEWS NET analysis, did not endorse their findings. They expressed concerns over the methodology employed in the analysis and suggested that "a reduction in acute malnutrition might also be considered possible" citing a greater food supply.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDevereux202411-142][^cite_note-143] The IPC found a decreasing risk of famine in their June assessment, but emphasized that the risk of famine remains high.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDevereux202410-141] The FRC, endorsing the IPC's findings, noted that Israel permitted an increasing amount of food into northern Gaza since March.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDevereux202412-144][^cite_note-145]

In June 2024, Cindy McCain, the head of the WFP, stated children and adults were dying from starvation after being reduced to the "size of a skeleton".[^cite_note-146] The same month, FEWS NET stated, "Regardless of whether or not the famine (IPC phase 5) thresholds have been definitively reached or exceeded, people are dying of hunger-related causes across Gaza".[^cite_note-147]

In late June, a leaked UN document said that 95% of the population of Gaza were in food insecurity, while almost 500,000 were facing near-famine hunger.[^cite_note-Leak-148] The report found famine remained a possibility throughout the entirety of the Gaza Strip, and that the risk was "as high" as at any other time during the conflict.[^cite_note-Hansler_2024-149][^cite_note-150] The UN stated one in five households went entire days without eating.[^cite_note-151] On 30 June 2024, the [IPC](16431152.md) Global Famine Review Committee said evidence indicated famine was not occurring in Gaza, but that high risk of famine would persist as long as the war and warned against complacency."[^cite_note-FRC-152] Israel has challenged the IPC's past methodology, citing academics in the Israeli public health sector.[^cite_note-timesofisrael20240531-153]

On 9 July 2024, a group of UN experts released a statement that Israel's "targeted starvation campaign" had caused the death of [children](75987631.md) in Gaza and that famine had spread from the North to the rest of Gaza. The statement cited the deaths of three children who had recently died of malnutrition in Gaza, saying: "When the first child dies from malnutrition and dehydration, it becomes irrefutable that famine has taken hold."[^cite_note-cnn20240709-154][^cite_note-cbsnews20240709-155][^cite_note-aljazeera20240709-156][^cite_note-ohchr20240709-157] According to Devereux, while there was no famine in Gaza "in the first half of 2024" according to the IPC's food insecurity scale, the situation in Gaza fell under other scholarly definitions which see it "as a process of intensifying food crisis," agreeing with the declarations of famine by FEWS NET and the WFP.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDevereux202415–16-158] WHO stated people were trapped in a "vicious cycle" as malnutrition was worsening people's vulnerability to diseases.[^cite_note-159] According to a group of UN experts, as of July 2024 Israel's "targeted starvation campaign" had spread throughout the entire Gaza Strip, causing the death of [children](75987631.md).[^cite_note-cnn20240709-154][^cite_note-cbsnews20240709-155][^cite_note-aljazeera20240709-156][^cite_note-ohchr20240709-157] The same month, detected cases of childhood malnutrition in northern Gaza increased by 300% compared to May 2024.[^cite_note-HSU200-160] In September 2024, [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International) warned that food conditions had "deteriorated badly" since May, stating, "There remains a grave risk of famine conditions spiraling once again."[^cite_note-161]

#### October–December 2024

Israel tightened the blockade in October,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDevereux202413-162] and individuals familiar with aid flow into the Gaza Strip stated that the amount of humanitarian aid entering the Strip was at its lowest levels in seven months.[^cite_note-163] The WFP stated on 11 October 2024 that no food had entered northern Gaza that entire month, and that one million people faced starvation.[^cite_note-UN_says_no_food-164] The [World Food Programme](33582.md) (WFP) warned in October 2024 that one million people were at risk of starvation.[^cite_note-UN_says_no_food-164] The human rights organizations [Gisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisha_\(human_rights_organization\)), [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem), [Physicians for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights%E2%80%93Israel) and [Yesh Din](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yesh_Din) warned this was one of the "alarming signs" that Israel was implementing the [General's Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General's_Plan_\(Israel-Hamas_War\)) to starve northern Gaza.[^cite_note-165] The United Nations warned on 17 October that it would run out of food for people in northern Gaza in just a week and a half.[^cite_note-166] The IPC released an updated report, finding nearly all people in Gaza faced high levels of acute food insecurity (Phase 3), and 133,000 people faced catastrophic lack of food (Phase 5).[^cite_note-167] By the end of the month, humanitarian aid to the Gaza Strip reached its lowest levels since the conflict began.[^cite_note-168]

In December 2024, OCHA claimed that only 2,205 aid trucks had entered Gaza, while Israel said 5,000 entered. The pre-war amount was about 15,000 a month. OCHA claimed that 91% of Gaza's population faced "high levels of acute food insecurity". Israel considered that month whether to limit more humanitarian aid after [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s [election](59568546.md).[^cite_note-169]

#### January–March 2025

During the [January 2025 Gaza war ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire), Israel permitted a maximum of 600 aid trucks to enter every day. The deliveries did not have any significant disruptions, and prices of food was lower.[^cite_note-170] On 5 February, the UN reported that over one million people had received food aid since the start of the ceasefire. In addition to food parcel deliveries, there were increased bread deliveries at bakeries and food kitchens. A community kitchen in northern Gaza had reopened on 24 January. The WFP delivered fuel, enabling five Gazan bakeries to boost production capacity by 40%.[^cite_note-171]

#### March – 9 October 2025

On 2 March 2025, Israel cut off all aid to the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-172] The blockade caused food prices to increase by up to 1,400%. By the end of the month, all bakeries supported by the WFP had run out of flour and cooking oil.[^cite_note-173] During this complete blockade (2–25 March), at least 58 people starved to death.[^cite_note-652025AJ-174][^cite_note-2552025IMEMC-175] In mid-April 2025, 12 CEOs of humanitarian organizations signed a statement that aid systems were in danger of collapsing.[^cite_note-NBC_News,_World_Food_Programme_has_run_out,_April_27,_2025-176]

On 25 April, the WFP announced that it had delivered its last remaining supplies to kitchens preparing hot meals in Gaza; the meals were expected to be gone within days. The kitchens had been the only consistent source of food assistance in the last few weeks, although they reached only half of the population with one quarter of their daily food requirements.[^cite_note-177] On 28 April, the Gaza Media Office announced that there were over 65,000 cases of acute malnutrition in children in the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-middleeasteye20250428-178] UNRWA corroborated these claims, saying there were 66,000 children suffering from severe malnutrition in May.[^cite_note-179]

According to a United Nations report from early June, 2,700 children under the age of five in Gaza were suffering from acute malnutrition, representing a threefold increase compared to three months earlier.[^cite_note-180]

In mid-July, it was reported that at least 76 children and 10 adults had died from malnutrition throughout the Gaza war.[^cite_note-2172025CNN-181] On 23 July, the Gaza Health Ministry reported that 10 people had died from starvation in the previous 24 hours; this was after reporting the previous day that 15 people had died in the 24 hours before that. This brought the total death count from starvation to 111.[^cite_note-2282025ABC-182][^cite_note-2382025AJ-183] On 26 July, the death toll rose to 122.[^cite_note-Hume2025-184]

The IPC reported on 25 July that a "worst-case scenario of famine" is unfolding in Gaza, citing additional displacement caused by [a new offensive in Deir al-Balah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_al-Balah_offensive), [mass killings near aid sites](80075155.md) operated by the [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md), and the lack of ready-to-eat items distributed by the GHF.[^cite_note-185] On 22 August, the IPC and the FRC confirmed famine within the [Gaza Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Governorate), warning that the classification could spread throughout Gaza.[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187] It concluded that [Deir al-Balah Governorate](14704596.md) and [Khan Yunis Governorate](13900254.md) would reach famine levels within a month. The IPC had insufficient data on [North Gaza Governorate](8689663.md) for a classification but concluded that conditions were likely similar or worse than in the Gaza Governorate.[^cite_note-IPC_22_August_2025-4][^cite_note-reuters20250822-12]

On 30 August, CNN reported that Israel had been dropping leaflets over Gaza City telling residents to move south of Wadi Gaza. However, the International Committee of the Red Cross said, "Such an evacuation would trigger a massive population movement that no area in the Gaza Strip can absorb, given the widespread destruction of civilian infrastructure and the extreme shortages of food, water, shelter and medical care."[^cite_note-CNN30August2025-188]

Since the GHF began operating in Gaza in May, at least 1,373 aid seekers have been killed, according to the UN Human Rights Office. Of these, 859 were near GHF-run aid sites, and 514 were killed while waiting along food convoy routes controlled by the IDF. Additionally, an MSF worker reported that Israeli forces attacked Palestinians who were following aid trucks.[^cite_note-189]

According to Democracy Now, the Palestinian academic Omar Harb starved to death at age 60 on 4 September.[^cite_note-190]

On 9 October 2025, UNRWA said they have enough food for the entrite population of the Gaza strip for 3 months.[^cite_note-191]

#### 10 October 2025 – present

The [ceasefire](81215079.md) between Israel and Hamas went into effect on 10 October 2025.[^cite_note-192] Under the ceasefire terms, Israel was to permit up to 600 humanitarian aid trucks to enter Gaza each day. Since then, the limit has been reduced to 300, with Israeli officials attributing the change to delays in recovering the bodies of Israeli hostages believed to be buried beneath rubble from Israeli strikes.[^cite_note-onworf-193] Data from the UN2720 Monitoring and Tracking Dashboard, which records the movement of humanitarian convoys entering Gaza, showed that between 10 and 16 October, only 216 trucks reached their intended destinations within Gaza.[^cite_note-onworf-193]

Since the ceasefire, Israel has increased the entry of medical and humanitarian supplies into Gaza; however, according to WHO, the amount remains far below what is required to restore the territory's health system.[^cite_note-194] On 23 October, 41 humanitarian organizations working in Gaza accused Israel of obstructing aid deliveries despite ceasefire commitments, citing arbitrary rejections of shipments and new restrictions on NGO registration. Between 10 and 21 October, 17 international NGOs were denied entry for essential supplies including food, water, and medicine, with 94% of all rejections issued to such organizations.[^cite_note-195]

On December 19, the IPC published a report, declaring that there is no longer a famine in Gaza after improvements in availability to commercial and humanitarian food delivery following the ceasefire, but warned that the situation remains "highly fragile".[^cite_note-apnews20251219-15] The IPC report also stated that more than 100,000 people faced catastrophic conditions, but projected that figure to decline to around 1,900 people by April 2026.[^cite_note-reuters20251219-6]

On 24 June 2026, Aziza al-Kahlout, spokesperson for the Palestinian Ministry of Social Development, said that the Gaza Strip was going through a critical phase due to a sharp decline in humanitarian aid deliveries in recent months, describing the situation as a "silent famine". She said living conditions had deteriorated significantly and that residents were facing increasing difficulty accessing food, clean water, and essential services. According to the ministry, approximately 14,242 aid trucks entered Gaza between January and the end of May 2026, compared with an expected 45,000 trucks based on planned daily aid deliveries, meaning that less than one-third of the anticipated aid had arrived.[^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197]

### Southern Gaza

On 7 January 2024, the [UNRWA](32270.md) deputy director reported severe hunger in southern Gaza, stating, "I don't know how much more they can bear before something explodes in the southern part of Gaza".[^cite_note-198] On 11 February, the mayor of [Rafah](584373.md) stated the city was facing famine and that available supplies were only enough for 10% of the population.[^cite_note-199] Long queues for food were reported in Rafah.[^cite_note-200] On 15 February, UNOCHA stated there was "an urgent need to establish a stabilisation centre in Rafah for treating children suffering from severe malnutrition".[^cite_note-201] UNOCHA stated on 17 February that people in Rafah were in "such dire need that they stop aid trucks to take food and eat it immediately".[^cite_note-202] On 19 February, Israeli gunboats fired at fishermen attempting to catch fish off the coast of Rafah.[^cite_note-203] A 73-year-old displaced woman stated on 25 February, "I have never witnessed the starvation like this... Death for us is much better than our current life".[^cite_note-204]

In May 2024, Israel launched a [military offensive in Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive), seizing and closing the [Rafah crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_crossing), one of two major crossings for humanitarian aid in Gaza.[^cite_note-205] Martin Griffiths, the UN's emergency relief coordinator, stated, "Civilians in Gaza are being starved and killed and we are prevented from helping them."[^cite_note-206] In an assessment report, UNOCHA stated the closure of Rafah crossing and Israel's assault would have "serious consequences" for food and nutrition services.[^cite_note-207] The UN ceased food distribution in Rafah on 21 May 2024, stating that humanitarian operations were on the verge of collapse, and if food supplies did not begin entering "in massive quantities, famine-like conditions will spread".[^cite_note-208] On 30 May, the WFP stated food access was so constrained that southern Gaza was at risk of the same levels of catastrophic hunger as the north.[^cite_note-un20240530-209] In June 2024, the WFP stated its stocked up supplies of food in southern Gaza were beginning to run out.[^cite_note-210] In July 2024, the WFP stated they had been forced to reduce food rations for families in central and southern Gaza due to limited supplies.[^cite_note-211] In September 2024, the UN warned that more than 1 million people in southern and central Gaza had received no food rations in the month of August.[^cite_note-212]

### Northern Gaza
