On 13 January 2024, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported that the WFP had stated that nine out of ten people in northern Gaza were eating less than a meal a day.[^cite_note-214] The [World Health Organization](33583.md) stated on 25 January 2024 that the food situation was "absolutely horrific" in northern Gaza, with rare aid deliveries mobbed by visibly starved people with sunken eyes.[^cite_note-215] A Mercy Corps team member reported he had witnessed such intense overcrowding of thousands around two food aid trucks in northern Gaza that two people suffocated to death.[^cite_note-216] A relief worker with Al Baraka, an Algerian charity, stated northern Gaza was on the verge of famine, saying, "Almost no relief aid has been delivered to the people here since the beginning of Israel's aggression."[^cite_note-217]

On 10 February 2024, the Gaza Media Office stated, "We immediately demand the entry of a thousand trucks daily into northern Gaza until it recovers from the famine".[^cite_note-218] On 15 February, *Al Jazeera* reported that people in northern Gaza were going days and even weeks without sufficient food.[^cite_note-219] The Food and Agriculture Organization stated that distributing food in northern Gaza remained a challenge as it was "barely accessible".[^cite_note-220] To survive, people ate animal feed, herbs, weeds, and grass.[^cite_note-221] A UNOCHA representative stated, "There are about 300,000 people in the north and I have no idea how they've survived".[^cite_note-222] In late February 2024, a grain mill in northern Gaza shut down due to a lack of fuel.[^cite_note-223]

On 20 February 2024, the WFP stated it would cease aid delivery to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-224] In response to the announcement, the Gaza Media Office stated it was "a death sentence for three-quarters of a million people".[^cite_note-225] On 24 February, UNRWA announced it was also suspending humanitarian services in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-226] Families in [Jabalia refugee camp](18169472.md) reported being so hungry they ate scraps, leaves, and their horses.[^cite_note-227] One man in Jabalia stated, "We have no water, no flour and we are very tired because of hunger."[^cite_note-228] *[Al Jazeera](30204052.md)* reported that two sisters searching for food on 25 February had been shot and claimed that Israeli Army was responsible for the incident.[^cite_note-229] Thousands of people in [Gaza City](55387.md) waited for a possible delivery of flour on 25 February.[^cite_note-230]

On 27 February 2024, the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) stated, "What is happening in northern Gaza is a true famine... This escalating famine could kill thousands of citizens due to malnutrition and dehydration in the coming days in front of the eyes of the world."[^cite_note-231] A man in the Jabalia refugee camp stated [children](75987631.md) "are dying and fainting in the streets from hunger. What can we do?"[^cite_note-232] On 17 March 2024, 13 aid trucks arrived in [Jabalia](453723.md) and Gaza City without incident, for the first time in months.[^cite_note-233] In late March 2024, the Integrated Food Security Phase Classification placed northern Gaza in Phase 5, its most severe stage.[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235] Analysis from the Food and Agriculture Organization found widespread destruction in Gaza's agricultural sector, including the deaths of 60% of milk cows, 70% of cattle stock, and 60% of small ruminants like goats and sheep.[^cite_note-236]

On 29 March 2024, the US State Department stated that famine in northern Gaza "quite possibly is present in at least some areas".[^cite_note-237] A UNOCHA spokesperson stated people faced "cruel death by famine".[^cite_note-238] By late March, the growing season for khobiza, a wild plant eaten in northern Gaza, was ending, which journalist Moath al-Kahlout stated "will lead to an even more horrific famine".[^cite_note-239] The UN stated 30% of its March 2024 aid missions had been denied.[^cite_note-240] In April 2024, [Oxfam](19698439.md) reported that people in northern Gaza were surviving on an average of 245 calories a day.[^cite_note-241]

On 3 May 2024, Cindy McCain, American director of the U.N. WFP, said "There is famine—full-blown famine—in the north, and it's moving its way south."[^cite_note-242] In early June 2024, the [Famine Early Warning Systems Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Famine_Early_Warning_Systems_Network) (FEWS NET) stated, "It is possible, if not likely" that famine was underway in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-243]

In late June, the FRC released a new analysis of the IPC projections in north Gaza. It challenged the IPC and FEWS NET's methodologies and said that current evidence did not prove that famine was imminent. However, it acknowledged the "extreme human suffering" of civilians in Gaza and called for sustainable humanitarian aid to enter. It also said that conditions in north Gaza had seemingly improved.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245][^cite_note-Hansler_2024-149] In response to the IPC findings, the WFP stated that "The improvement shows the difference that greater access can make" and "Increased food deliveries to the north and nutrition services have helped to reduce the very worst levels of hunger, leaving a still desperate situation."[^cite_note-246] In July 2024, UNOCHA stated commercial trucks had not been able to make deliveries to northern Gaza for several months.[^cite_note-247] In August 2024, the United Nations reported that childhood malnutrition cases had increased 300% between May and July 2024.[^cite_note-248]

In November 2024, amidst the [Siege of North Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_North_Gaza), the [Inter-Agency Standing Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-Agency_Standing_Committee) stated, "The entire Palestinian population in North Gaza is at imminent risk of dying from disease, famine and violence."[^cite_note-249] On 8 November, the Famine Review Committee that famine was "imminent in areas within the Northern Gaza Strip." It also warned that action would need to be taken "within days not weeks" to avoid the situation.[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251]

## Humanitarian aid

See also: [Humanitarian aid during the Gaza war](76066918.md)



March 2024 US airdrop of aid into Gaza. [Al Jazeera](30204052.md) reported that the drop was described as "ineffective" by [Oxfam](19698439.md), and "symbolic" by a former director of [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID).

[^cite_note-252]

[^cite_note-253]

On 9 January 2024, Gisha reported that only 6,000 aid trucks had entered Gaza since 7 October, the equivalent of 12 days of aid before the conflict's start.[^cite_note-254] Colonel Moshe Tetro, who heads the Israeli unit overseeing deliveries of [humanitarian aid](77142854.md), stated that there was no food shortage in Gaza and that existing reserves are sufficient.[^cite_note-Burke-255] Another Israeli official stated, "Don't forget that this is an Arab, Gazan population whose DNA is to hoard, certainly when it comes to food."[^cite_note-256]

Officials stated that the worsening crisis was partly attributable to the limited amount of aid being allowed into Gaza, with Cindy McCain stating, "People in Gaza risk dying of hunger just miles from trucks filled with food".[^cite_note-257] Arif Husain, the WFP chief economist, stated on 24 January that only between 20 and 30% of needed aid was entering Gaza,[^cite_note-258] as UNOCHA accused Israel of "systematically denying" humanitarian assistance into northern Gaza.[^cite_note-259]

On 1 February 2024, United Nations Secretary-General António Guterres called for unrestricted humanitarian access, stating, "Everyone in Gaza is hungry."[^cite_note-260] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) stated that the decision of 18 countries to [defund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNRWA_October_7_controversy) [UNRWA](32270.md) risked hastening famine.[^cite_note-261] The WFP stated on 2 February that aid to northern Gaza was being overwhelmingly rejected by the Israelis.[^cite_note-262] Journalist Abubaker Abed stated, "Families eat strategically, just to stay alive."[^cite_note-263]

While speaking to *CNN* reporters in February 2024, some Palestinians stated humanitarian aid was being resold on the black market, with packages already opened. Israeli airstrikes around certain areas also caused prices to spike, with a 25-kilogram bag of flour jumping from \$20 in Kahn Younis to \$34 after intensified airstrikes.[^cite_note-cnn20240130-115] The same month, Human Rights Watch criticized the defunding of UNRWA, which they termed "the main humanitarian channel into Gaza", in the face of "mounting risks of famine and a binding order by the World Court in a case about genocide".[^cite_note-264]

On 13 February 2024, Finance Minister [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md) blocked a US-funded flour shipment to Gaza and stated he had done so "in coordination with the prime minister".[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266] White House National Security Adviser Jack Sullivan confirmed Israel was blocking flour from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-267] On 14 February, the *[Financial Times](136566.md)* reported that an aid shipment that could have fed more than 1 million people for a month had been blocked at the Israeli [port of Ashdod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Ashdod), with the Israeli government stating the food would not be released.[^cite_note-268] The UN stated aid deliveries had halved in February from the month before.[^cite_note-269] On 28 February, USAID chief [Samantha Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samantha_Power) stated more aid needed to enter Gaza, calling the situation "a matter of life and death".[^cite_note-270] Following a US announcement that it was beginning [airdrops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airdrop) of aid and building a temporary port off the coast of Gaza, Michael Fakhri, the UN Special Rapporteur on the Right to Food, stated, "The time when countries use air drops and these maritime piers is usually, if not always, in situations when you want to deliver humanitarian aid into enemy territory".[^cite_note-271]

In March 2024, there were efforts to address the crisis include attempts to negotiate a humanitarian aid cease-fire and allow for the provision of aid in addition to a hostage release deal.[^cite_note-washingtonpost20240304-272] In March, the US began airdropping meals into Gaza. The first two aid drops provided over 74,800 US and Jordanian meals.[^cite_note-273] The [International Committee of the Red Cross](5023832.md) stated in late March: "Some families receive a can of food every other day for the whole family".[^cite_note-274] In late March, residents reported that animal feed was running out in parts of Gaza.[^cite_note-275] In late April 2024, the WFP stated half of Gaza's population is starving.[^cite_note-276] At the end of May 2024, the United Nations stated that humanitarian aid deliveries had dropped 67% since the start of Israel's Rafah offensive at the beginning of the month.[^cite_note-un20240530-209] The WFP stated it was unable to feed most civilians in Rafah and described conditions as "apocalyptic".[^cite_note-277]

In June 2024, the WFP stated that aid workers moving through the Kerem Shalom crossing faced risks due to "fighting, damaged roads, unexploded ordnance, and Israeli restrictions."[^cite_note-278] [World Central Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen) said it had provided over 50 million meals in Gaza with plans to expand operations.[^cite_note-279] By the end of June 2024, almost no aid was entering into Gaza.[^cite_note-280] As of July 2024, hundreds of trucks were waiting to enter into Gaza, some having been delayed for as long as two months.[^cite_note-281] In September 2024, the [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council) reported that 83% of required food aid was failing to enter the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-282]

### Gaza floating pier

On 7 March 2024, President Biden announced plans to build a [Gaza floating pier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_floating_pier) to deliver food and humanitarian aid. The pier's cost was an estimated \$320 million.[^cite_note-283] At its closure announcement on 17 July, the pier had been operational for 20 days, delivering 8,800 tonnes (19,400,000 lb) of aid. The pier had been dismantled three times because of high [sea states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_state).[^cite_note-cnn-20240717-284]

### Gaza Humanitarian Foundation

This section is an excerpt from [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation\&action=edit)]

The [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md) (GHF) was an organization backed by the [Israeli](72545811.md) and [American governments](78298108.md) with the stated purpose of distributing [humanitarian aid](434679.md) amid the ongoing famine and [humanitarian crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_humanitarian_crisis_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)) resulting from the [Israeli blockade of the Gaza Strip](75013719.md).[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_bbc20250509-285] It was established in February 2025, suspended operations in October 2025, and closed in November 2025.[^cite_note-286] Intended to bypass the [United Nations](32270.md) as the main supplier of aid in Gaza,[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_BBC_30_June-287] GHF began operating in May 2025 as a response to fraudulent[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_unn-288][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_nyju-289] Israeli claims that aid was being routinely diverted by [Hamas](13913.md). Aid organizations including the UN have denied these claims[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_unn-288][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_nyju-289][^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292] and attributed the aid theft to Palestinian groups armed by the Israeli military.[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_theguardian20250605-293][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_theguardian20250611-294] The GHF has been criticized by the [United Nations](31769.md) and humanitarian groups for politicizing aid distribution, with established humanitarian groups saying the GHF is giving cover for Israel to pursue its aims to depopulate [Palestinians](23267.md) from the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) and use [starvation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starvation_\(crime\)) as a weapon of war.[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_aljazeera20250602-296][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_aljazeera20250601-297]

There were [repeated mass killings in the vicinity of GHF distribution sites](80075155.md). As of 15 August 2025, the [UN Office of the High Commissioner for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Office_of_the_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights) (OHCHR) reported that of the 1,760 Palestinians killed since 27 May while seeking food, 994 were killed in the vicinity of GHF sites.[^cite_note-298] The attacks at GHF sites were attributed to [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) by the OHCHR,[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] Gaza officials,[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_cbc20250625-302][^cite_note-303] and witnesses.[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_cbc20250625-302][^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306] US contractors with the GHF were also filmed shooting Palestinian civilians, and according to former staff, its organizational culture dehumanized aid seekers and imposed few controls on the behavior of contractors.[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_bbcjul-307][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_apjul-308] The GHF and the IDF repeatedly denied responsibility for deaths at aid sites, saying that only warning shots were fired.[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_npr20250723-309][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_dw20250703-310][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_pbs20250712-311] Survivors of the repeated mass killings perpetrated at the distribution sites began to refer to the Israeli-backed operation as traps or death traps rather than aid.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) described the conditions around these distributions as "slaughter masquerading as aid"[^cite_note-314] and "orchestrated killing".[^cite_note-315] [The Center for Constitutional Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Constitutional_Rights) and 14 other human rights organizations warned that the GHF could be liable for [complicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complicity_in_genocide) in [war crimes](48626.md), [crimes against humanity](61099.md), and [genocide against Palestinians](75668427.md).[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_theguardian20250623-316] Critics referred to the mass killings at GHF sites as the "hunger games" in reference to the American [media franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hunger_Games_\(franchise\)).[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320]

The [United Nations](31769.md) and other aid groups refused to cooperate with the GHF and accused them of politicizing and "weaponizing aid", and delivering it in a manner that is unsafe and degrading for the Palestinian people.[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_aljazeera20250602-296][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_aljazeera20250601-297][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_bbc-321][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_guardian-322][^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_theguardian20250623-316] The United Nations and over 170 charities and [NGOs](46539.md), including [Save the Children](436885.md) and [Oxfam](19698439.md), accused the GHF of failing to uphold[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_aljazeera20250527-323] and violating humanitarian norms by forcing two million Palestinians into overcrowded and militarized zones and subjecting aid-seekers to almost daily attacks; they demanded that the GHF be immediately closed.[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_charities-condemned-GHF-324]

GHF was led by executive director John Acree,[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_aljazeera20250612-325] a former [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID) manager, and executive chairman [Johnnie Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnnie_Moore_Jr.), an [American evangelical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_evangelical) leader and businessman.[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_theguardian20250603-326] GHF's operations were suspended following the [October 2025 Gaza ceasefire](81215079.md#Components). A spokesperson for the group described an intent to resume operations.[^cite_note-Gaza_Humanitarian_Foundation_BBCsuspends-327] In November 2025, the GHF announced the permanent end of its operations in Gaza, saying that they "succeeded in their mission of showing there's a better way to deliver aid to Gazans."[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-329]

### Israeli blocking of aid



Israelis blocking humanitarian aid from entering Gaza, February 2024

See also: [Israeli blockade of the Gaza Strip (2023–present)](75013719.md), [Gaza war protests in Israel § Protest against humanitarian aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_Israel#Protest_against_humanitarian_aid), [Israeli direct action against aid delivery to Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_direct_action_against_aid_delivery_to_Gaza), and [UNRWA and Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNRWA_and_Israel)

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Humanitarian aid during the Gaza war § Israeli blocking of aid](76066918.md#Israeli_blocking_of_aid).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Humanitarian_aid_during_the_Gaza_war\&action=edit)]

Before being allowed entry into Gaza, humanitarian aid trucks are put through stringent inspections by Israel. Janti Soeripto, president and chief executive of Save the Children, explained to reporters the convoluted regulations that cause items to be turned back, like [sanitary pads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menstrual_pad) due to a [nail clipper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nail_clipper) included in the hygiene kit, or sleeping bags because they had zippers. A humanitarian official in contact with the Israeli COGAT unit has raised claims that the rejections are due to the unit operating with only an obsolete list from 2008 and a lack of official guidance.[^cite_note-Humanitarian_aid_during_the_Gaza_war_cnn20240301-330] Others, such as the [European Union](9317.md)'s top diplomat, [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell), have accused Israel of intentionally using starvation as a weapon of war.[^cite_note-331] Israel also does not allow humanitarian aid bound for Gaza to be purchased in Israel or the West Bank, or to use Israel ports.[^cite_note-Humanitarian_aid_during_the_Gaza_war_Tanis-332] It has also closed all but one of the Israel-Gaza checkpoints.[^cite_note-Humanitarian_aid_during_the_Gaza_war_Tanis-332] Egyptian Foreign Minister [Sameh Shoukry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sameh_Shoukry) stated that "Israeli obstacles" were impeding the delivery of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-333] The [World Health Organization](33583.md) stated their "requests to deliver supplies are often blocked or refused" by Israel.[^cite_note-334] [Antonio Guterres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Guterres), the UN secretary-general, stated that alleviating conditions in Gaza "requires Israel removing the remaining obstacles and chokepoints to relief".[^cite_note-335] A September 2024 investigation found that [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Agency_for_International_Development) and other agencies found Israel that deliberately [blocked humanitarian aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blocking_of_humanitarian_aid), but the U.S. Secretary of State rejected their findings.[^cite_note-336]

An investigation found that Israel was blocking cancer medications, sleeping bags, drinking water purification tablets, and maternity kits from entering Gaza, leading the [Save the Children](436885.md) US president to state she had "never seen anything like the level of barriers being put in place to hamper humanitarian assistance".[^cite_note-Humanitarian_aid_during_the_Gaza_war_cnn20240301-330] MP [Rosena Allin-Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosena_Allin-Khan) stated that Israel had prevented water filters from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-337] The UN reported Israel had rejected aid trucks for carrying medical scissors.[^cite_note-338] On 6 March, Israel continued to block a major US shipment of flour from entering Gaza, after having already blocked it from entering for 46 days.[^cite_note-339] On 7 March 2024, Israeli police blocked an aid convoy organized by the Jewish-Arab cooperation group [Standing Together](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standing_Together_\(movement\)).[^cite_note-340]

The president of the [American Near East Refugee Aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Near_East_Refugee_Aid) stated, "Some governments—not just the US but others as well—who thought that they would make more headway with the Israelis on land aid threw up their hands and said, 'Well, let's do airdrops.'"[^cite_note-341] In an interview with the *New Yorker*, US Senator [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) stated, "Administration officials have recognized that the Netanyahu government has put up unacceptable barriers to the delivery of humanitarian assistance into Gaza".[^cite_note-342] [Catherine Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_M._Russell), the chair of [UNICEF](19698110.md) described Israel's bureaucratic approval system as [Kafkaesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafkaesque).[^cite_note-343] In March, UNRWA said that Israel was no longer allowing the agency to deliver aid to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-344] [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths) stated, "The decision to block its food convoys to the north only pushes thousands closer to famine. It must be revoked."[^cite_note-345] The EU's Josep Borrell stated, "Enough food for the people starving in Gaza is stockpiled and waiting to enter through land routes".[^cite_note-346]

[Gisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisha_\(human_rights_organization\)), an Israeli human rights organization, stated Israel was blocking [dual-use items](1003982.md) and that it had petitioned the Israeli Supreme Court to order Israel to meets its obligations to civilians in Gaza.[^cite_note-347] In April 2024, the [Freedom Flotilla Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Flotilla_Coalition) stated [a scheduled aid trip to Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Gaza_Freedom_Flotilla) had been delayed by an administrative roadblock "initiated by Israel in an attempt to prevent our departure."[^cite_note-348] A [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Agency_for_International_Development) internal assessment reportedly found Israel "does not currently demonstrate necessary compliance" with a requirement that countries not impede "the transport of delivery of United States humanitarian assistance".[^cite_note-349] In July 2024, 13 human rights organizations, including Oxfam, Doctors Without Borders, Save the Children and the Norwegian Refugee Council, stated Israel was blocking humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-350] UNICEF spokesperson James Elder stated that Israel was intentionally restricting aid from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-351]

In September 2024, the [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council) reported that 83% of required food aid was failing to enter the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-352] In October 2024, humanitarian aid deliveries dropped to their lowest levels in seven months, according to individuals involved with aid.[^cite_note-353] After the United States told Israel it could face consequences unless it increased aid to northern Gaza, Israeli officials stated they would hold an "emergency meeting" regarding humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-354] Soon after, the Israeli Knesset [passed a bill barring contact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_ban_on_UNRWA?action=edit\&redlink=1) between [UNRWA](32270.md) and Israeli officials within three months, which is necessary for UNRWA to coordinate transferring aid into the Gaza Strip, due to Israel controlling all crossings.[^cite_note-355] The United States responded by saying they had not seen enough progress by Israel to increase humanitarian aid deliveries.[^cite_note-356] Several days later, the U.S. stated it would "follow the law" if Israel did not comply with its recommendation to allow in humanitarian aid by its deadline.[^cite_note-357]

In November 2024, it was reported that Israel allowed limited delivery of aid to [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun), in northern Gaza, but before the aid arrived, the Israeli army forced inhabitants to evacuate the area or risk being harmed in airstrikes.[^cite_note-358]

In February 2025, according to the Israeli Defense Ministry, a truck carrying aid through the Erez corridor was searched and found to be smuggling hundreds of smartphones, and other electronics. The driver was subsequently arrested.[^cite_note-359]

### Israeli attacks on aid distribution

Main article: [2025 Gaza Strip aid distribution killings](80075155.md)

According to [Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture), a research group at [Goldsmiths, University of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goldsmiths,_University_of_London), more than 40 Israeli attacks on humanitarian aid seekers have been documented. According to the group, these were not isolated events, but were rather "systematic in nature".[^cite_note-360]

[Human Rights Watch](46530.md) reported eight incidents since the start of the war until May 2024 in which aid workers were attacked by Israeli forces despite providing locations for Israeli leaders. The eight incidents, it said, reveal serious flaws in the so-called "conflict prevention mechanism" – a mechanism designed to protect aid workers and allow them to safely deliver life-saving humanitarian aid to the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-HSU200-160]

On 25 January 2024, the Gaza Health Ministry reported an Israeli attack on aid seekers had killed 20 and wounded 150.[^cite_note-361] Israel bombed a truck loaded with food head toward northern Gaza on 5 February.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] On 6 February, Israeli forces reportedly opened fire on people waiting for food aid trucks in Gaza City.[^cite_note-364] UNOCHA stated it was the fifth report of Israeli firing upon people waiting for humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-365] On 18 February, multiple instances of Israeli sniper attacks on civilians seeking humanitarian assistance were reported.[^cite_note-366] On 20 February, at least one Palestinian civilian was killed while waiting to receive humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-367] People seeking aid were attacked by Israeli forces on multiple occasions.[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369] UNOCHA stated on 27 February that "aid convoys have come under fire and are systematically denied access to people in need".[^cite_note-370] On 28 February, medical sources in Gaza City reported three people were killed while waiting for aid on [al-Rashid Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Rashid_Street_\(Gaza\)).[^cite_note-371]

On 29 February, more than 100 people seeking humanitarian died in the [Flour Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_Massacre) by Israeli gunfire at the Al Nabulsi roundabout west of Gaza City.[^cite_note-372] The United Nations called for an investigation on 1 March into the killing of humanitarian aid seekers, stating it had "recorded at least 14 incidents involving shooting and shelling of people gathered to receive desperately needed supplies".[^cite_note-373]

On 2 March, three people in [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun) were killed while picking herbs for food.[^cite_note-374] On 3 March, at least nine people were killed while waiting for humanitarian aid in an Israeli airstrike in Deir el-Balah.[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376] Later on the same day, dozens of civilians were killed in an Israeli attack on aid seekers at the Kuwaiti roundabout in Gaza City.[^cite_note-377] The Ministry of Health called it a "horrific massacre".[^cite_note-378] On 4 March, another attack was reported at the Kuwaiti roundabout, after thousands of people waited all day for humanitarian aid, Israeli soldiers opened fire on them as soon as the trucks arrived.[^cite_note-Another_Israeli_attack-379] Al Jazeera stated the attacks on aid seekers had become "a near-daily occurrence".[^cite_note-Another_Israeli_attack-379] On 6 March, eight people were wounded after Israel fired live rounds at people seeking humanitarian aid at the Nabulsi roundabout.[^cite_note-380] On 7 March, five people were killed while waiting for aid at the Nabulsi roundabout.[^cite_note-381] On 8 March, several people seeking humanitarian aid were reportedly killed by Israeli open fire at the Kuwait Roundabout.[^cite_note-382] By 12 March, Israel had killed an estimated 400 humanitarian aid seekers in Gaza.[^cite_note-383] Israeli attacks on humanitarian aid seekers was described as the "new normal" for Palestinians in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-384]

On 13 March 2024, at least nine aid seekers were shot and wounded by Israeli troops at the Kuwaiti roundabout.[^cite_note-385] On 14 March 2024, Israeli troops [fired on Palestinians receiving aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Roundabout_mass_killings) at the Kuwaiti roundabout, killing 21 and injuring more than 150 others.[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] In a statement on 15 March, the UN humanitarian aid chief Martin Griffiths stated that attacks on aid seekers "cannot be allowed to continue".[^cite_note-388] The [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council) stated the attacks "shouldn't be happening".[^cite_note-389] On 19 March, at least 23 people were killed when Israeli fighter jets targeted a group of aid coordinators at the Kuwaiti roundabout.[^cite_note-390]

On 1 April 2024, an [Israeli drone fired three consecutive missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen_aid_convoy_attack) at three cars belonging to the [World Central Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen) (WCK), killing seven aid workers who had been distributing food in the northern Gaza Strip, which has been pushed close to famine by [Israel's siege](75208470.md) and blockade during the Israel–Hamas war.[^cite_note-ap1-391] In June 2024, eight people were reportedly killed at an UNRWA aid distribution training center near Gaza City.[^cite_note-392]

From May 2025[^cite_note-393] [GHF](79923808.md) was set up as the sole aid distributor in Gaza but repeated and regular mass shootings and killings at the distribution sites means that they have been described as "slaughter masquerading as aid".[^cite_note-394]

Survivors of the repeated mass killings perpetrated at the distribution sites have called this Israeli-backed operation "traps" rather than aid.[^cite_note-395][^cite_note-theguardian20250623-396] [The Center for Constitutional Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Constitutional_Rights) and 14 other human rights organizations have warned that the GHF could be liable for [complicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complicity_in_genocide) in [war crimes](48626.md), [crimes against humanity](61099.md), and [genocide against Palestinians](75668427.md).[^cite_note-theguardian20250623-396]

Members of the IDF and of the Israeli police have been providing information to far right Israeli settlers about upcoming aid truck convoys delivering food to Gaza so they could intercept the deliveries to vandalize and destroy them. A spokesperson for the radical right Israeli group [Tzav 9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzav_9) stated that the group has been blocking aid trucks to Gaza since January 2024, acknowledged receiving advance information from Israeli forces but denied being the ones who burned trucks. Videos taken in May 2024 show Israelis intercepting aid trucks and destroying food aid. Many Israeli and Palestinian witnesses reported that the IDF soldiers sent to accompany and protect the aid trucks watched as the settlers attacked the convoys but did nothing to protect them.[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398]

### Looting

Main article: [Societal breakdown in the Gaza Strip during the Gaza war](77392232.md)

In February 2024, *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported that [lawlessness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawlessness) in Gaza was hindering aid efforts.[^cite_note-399] *[Axios](52436164.md)* reported that armed gangs have been attacking and looting aid trucks since Hamas police have quit due to Israeli attacks.[^cite_note-400] A [Palestine Red Crescent Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Red_Crescent_Society) spokesman stated that the [civil disorder](329906.md) "contributed to around a 50% decrease in the total number of aid trucks entering Gaza in February" and an Egyptian aid truck driver described people climbing and smashing aid trucks.[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402]

In June, *[The New York Times](30680.md)* reported that relief groups had stopped delivering aid to southern Gaza due to looting and attacks from armed gangs, with aid trucks being peppered by bullet holes. Both commercial and aid agencies decided that they could not risk employees' lives. One aid worker described the daily attacks from armed criminal gangs in the Israel-Gaza border area as being coordinated and organized. The worker said that sometimes the aid truck drivers were beaten.[^cite_note-nytimes20240619-403] [AP News](18935732.md) spoke with an UN official who described thousands of aid trucks piled up, armed groups regularly obstructing convoys, and drivers being held at gunpoint.[^cite_note-apnews20240620-404] A worker at a Palestinian trucking company said that aid was spoiling in the hot weather. To try to make up for the aid deficiency, Israel allowed more commercial trucks into Gaza from Israel and the occupied West Bank, which unlike UN convoys, usually travel with armed protection. One Gazan businessman said that in the past he paid thousands of dollars to other Gazans to protect his trucks.[^cite_note-nytimes20240619-403] An associate professor of political science at Al-Azhar University said the lawlessness is a result of increasing desperation and the power vacuum left from Hamas' decreasing power over Gaza.[^cite_note-apnews20240620-404]

In late June, the UN warned that it would suspend aid operations in Gaza unless Israel increased efforts to protect humanitarian workers. A State Department spokesman said that in June, looting and other criminal attacks were the largest barriers to delivering aid, rather than Israeli strikes or Hamas' commandeering of aid convoys.[^cite_note-405] In July, the UN said that they would be bringing in more personal safety equipment and armored vehicles following approval from Israeli officials.[^cite_note-406]

On 16 November 2024 armed gangs captured and looted 98 trucks carrying aid. The convoy, which had been organized by UNRWA and the WFP, consisted of 109 trucks and was attacked shortly after crossing into Gaza from [Kerem Shalom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom). A UNRWA spokesperson said that "We're back at a stage where we're seeing people literally fighting over a bag of flour" and that "Israeli authorities continue to restrict a huge amount of the humanitarian response. Everything here is being strangled—food, flour, water—everything."[^cite_note-407] One UN aid official blamed organized crime for attacks on aid convoys, and others have reported that family clans from southern Gaza previously known to be involved with looting were behind the most recent attacks on convoys. UN aid officials have said that delivering aid has never been more difficult.[^cite_note-408] According to a UN memo, the criminals would operate with "the passive, if not active benevolence" of the IDF.[^cite_note-409]

## Effect on children and elderly

### Children



Girl in Gaza on her way to get food (August 2024).

See also: [Effect of the Gaza war on children in the Gaza Strip](75987631.md) and [Death of Yazan al-Kafarneh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Yazan_al-Kafarneh)

On 14 January 2024, Philippe Lazzarini stated, "Whenever you go to a school, the kids are looking at your eyes begging for a sip of water or a loaf of bread."[^cite_note-410] On 16 January, officials reported newborn babies with undernourished mothers were dying within days, and children weakened by starvation were dying from [hypothermia](146879.md).[^cite_note-Burke-255] On 18 January, the deputy executive director of UNICEF took a tour of the Gaza Strip, stating he had witnessed "some of the most horrific conditions I have ever seen" and that "thousands of children are malnourished and sick."[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412] On 10 February 2024, a UNICEF spokesperson said Gaza had the world's highest rate of child malnutrition.[^cite_note-413] On 17 February, the [Euro-Mediterranean Human Rights Monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro-Mediterranean_Human_Rights_Monitor) reported an instance of an eight-year-old girl who had died from starvation and dehydration.[^cite_note-414]

On 19 February 2024, UNICEF found that nearly 16% of children in northern Gaza under two years old were "[acutely malnourished](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Acute_Malnutrition)", with 3% suffering from severe [wasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wasting).[^cite_note-415] One mother in northern Gaza described the situation on 21 February, stating, "My little one wakes up at night screaming from hunger".[^cite_note-416] On 26 February, two children from Gaza City stated they ate once every two days, and were then eating animal feed.[^cite_note-417] [Action Aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_Aid), citing the head of pediatrics at Kamal Adwan Hospital, stated on 25 February that a "significant number" of children in northern Gaza had already died from starvation.[^cite_note-418] At least six children died of malnutrition on 28 February.[^cite_note-419] [Project HOPE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_HOPE), an aid organization in Deir el-Balah, stated 11% of the under-five-year-olds they had seen were experiencing malnutrition.[^cite_note-420]

A doctor at Kamal Adwan Hospital stated they had seen a steep rise in pediatric malnutrition cases.[^cite_note-421] On 28 February, a representative from Save the Children stated that due to [Israeli bombardment](76139149.md) and restrictions on aid, children were starving with trucks full of food waiting to enter into Gaza, describing this as "the killing of children in slow motion".[^cite_note-422] A two-year-old died from [food poisoning](531611.md) after eating bread made from animal feed.[^cite_note-423] Four more children died of starvation on 29 February, bringing the week's total to at least ten.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] Melanie Ward, the director of Medical Aid for Palestinians, stated, "This is the fastest decline in a population's nutrition status ever recorded. That means children are being starved at the fastest rate the world has ever seen."[^cite_note-426] On 3 March, health officials stated at least 15 children at Kamal Adwan Hospital had died of malnutrition and dehydration in the preceding few days.[^cite_note-427] [CNN](62028.md) reported that the true number could be higher, with a UNICEF representative stating there were likely more starving children "fighting for their lives" in other parts of Gaza.[^cite_note-Newborns_die_of_hunger-428][^cite_note-429]

[Yazan al-Kafarneh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Yazan_al-Kafarneh), a 10-year-old boy who died from starvation, was described as "the face of starvation in Gaza".[^cite_note-430] The father of a 5-year-old who died from starvation stated, "He did not suffer from any disease. My child died before my eyes because of hunger".[^cite_note-431] In June 2024, children died of malnutrition as Israel's Rafah offensive caused sharp reductions in humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-432] The Gaza Media Office stated on 3 June 2024 that 3,500 children under the age of five were at risk of death from starvation.[^cite_note-433] UNICEF placed the number at almost 3,000 children.[^cite_note-434] The World Health Organization stated 8,000 children had been diagnosed with malnutrition.[^cite_note-435] In late June 2024, a mother struggling to feed her children stated, "We are living the worst days of our lives in terms of famine and deprivation".[^cite_note-436]

On 28 April 2025, the Gaza Media Office announced that there were over 65,000 cases of acute malnutrition in children in the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-middleeasteye20250428-178] On 25 May, a four-year-old child named Mohammad Mustafa Yassin died from prolonged malnutrition according to doctors at the [Al-Ahli Arab Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital). His death brings the number of Palestinians who have died from malnutrition due to the blockade in Gaza to 58.[^cite_note-437]

#### Infants and newborns

A two-month-old baby died in Gaza City on 24 February from malnutrition.[^cite_note-438] A pediatrician working in Gaza stated, "Nursing mothers are unable to lactate as their health worsens".[^cite_note-439] The head of pediatrics at Kamal Adwan Hospital stated the hospital was struggling to find milk to feed infant patients, stating, "Even newborns are emaciated".[^cite_note-440] The [UN Population Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Population_Fund) stated doctors "no longer see normal-sized babies" but instead more stillborns.[^cite_note-441] In March 2024, a doctor at the Emirati Hospital stated 16 [premature babies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_Strip_preterm_births) had died from malnutrition-related diseases in the past five weeks.[^cite_note-442] The United Nations reported that one-third of children under two were "acutely malnourished" by 16 March.[^cite_note-443] Catherine M. Russell, the head of UNICEF, stated, "I have been in wards where babies are suffering from malnutrition, the whole ward is absolutely quiet because the babies don't even have the energy to cry."[^cite_note-444] [WHO](33583.md) stated, "What doctors and medical staff are telling us is more and more they are seeing the effects of starvation; they're seeing newborn babies simply dying because they (are) too low birth weight".[^cite_note-445] On 15 March, UNICEF reported that acute malnutrition amongst under-two-year-olds had doubled in one month.[^cite_note-446]

In late March 2024, doctors at Kamal Adwan Hospital stated babies were dying from hunger and dehydration.[^cite_note-447] By June 2024, newborns and infants starved due to the lack of available [infant formula](65330.md) and mothers' inability to [breastfeed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breastfeed).[^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449] In July 2024, ActionAid stated that according to their partners, "Pregnant women \[are] losing their babies because they are so malnourished".[^cite_note-450]

### Elderly

In March 2024, the elderly were reportedly dying at an "alarmingly high rate", with the Euro-Med Monitor stating it was receiving daily reports of the elderly dying of starvation.[^cite_note-451] The group stated, "The majority of these cases do not reach hospital... after dying at home, the elderly are buried either close to their residences or in makeshift graves".[^cite_note-452]

On 18 December 2023, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) accused Israel of "using starvation of civilians as a method of warfare in the occupied Gaza Strip".[^cite_note-hrw20231218-69] On 16 January 2024, UN experts accused Israel of "destroying Gaza's food system and using food as a weapon against the Palestinian people".[^cite_note-ohchr20240116-111] The [United Nations Special Rapporteur on the Right to Food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Special_Rapporteur_on_the_Right_to_Food), [Michael Fakhri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Fakhri), stated Israel was using hunger as a weapon against Palestinians.[^cite_note-453] On 23 January, [Alex De Waal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_De_Waal) stated Israel was committing a war crime through enforced starvation, stating, "An entire population being reduced to this stage is really unprecedented. We haven't seen it in Ethiopia, in Sudan and Yemen – pretty much anywhere else in the world."[^cite_note-454]

On 13 February 2024, US Senator [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) stated, "Kids in Gaza are now dying from the deliberate withholding of food. That is a war crime. It is a textbook war crime. And that makes those who orchestrate it war criminals."[^cite_note-455] A representative from the Palestinian non-profit organization [Juhoud for Community and Rural Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juhoud_for_Community_and_Rural_Development?action=edit\&redlink=1) stated, "The denial of access to food, water, and other necessities consists of a serious violation of international law".[^cite_note-456] [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell), [High Representative of the European Union for Foreign Affairs and Security Policy](750942.md), said in an interview with *[El Pais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Pais)* that "We are already in the midst of a catastrophe. The United Nations has had to suspend humanitarian aid: Israel is using famine as a weapon of war and that is contrary to international law."[^cite_note-457] On 3 March, Amnesty International head [Agnes Callamard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnes_Callamard) stated that the death of ten children from malnutrition that week was unlawful and the result of an Israeli "engineered famine".[^cite_note-458]

The [Palestinian Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_and_Expatriates_\(State_of_Palestine\)) accused Israel of violating international law by "using starvation as a weapon of war".[^cite_note-459] [Physicians for Human Rights–Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights%E2%80%93Israel) stated "According to the International Criminal Court, starvation is considered a war crime. Israel must immediately... stop limiting life-saving humanitarian aid. This is a moral stain that will stay with us for generations."[^cite_note-460] Belgian foreign minister [Alexander De Croo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_De_Croo) stated Israel was engaging in "tactics of starvation".[^cite_note-461] The global humanitarian director of [Plan International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plan_International) stated, "Israeli restrictions on aid... must cease immediately. The starvation of the civilian population is illegal under international humanitarian law."[^cite_note-462] Jeremy Laurence, the [UN Human Rights Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Human_Rights_Office) spokesman, stated Israel's conduct may constitute "starvation as a method of war, which is a war crime".[^cite_note-463]

[Ayman Safadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayman_Safadi), the Foreign Minister of Jordan, stated, "Israel is weaponizing starvation. This is another horrific war crime."[^cite_note-464] The Foreign Minister of the Republic of Ireland [Micheál Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miche%C3%A1l_Martin) stated, "The use of starvation as a weapon of war is a blatant violation of international humanitarian law."[^cite_note-465] [Caroline Gennez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Gennez), Belgium's minister of development cooperation, stated Israel's use of hunger as weapon of war was a "flagrant violation of international law".[^cite_note-466] A June 2024 investigation by *[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent)* found both current and former officials believed the United States was complicit in the creation of famine in Gaza.[^cite_note-467] In July 2024, a group of ten UN special rapporteurs stated Israel had conducted a "targeted starvation campaign" which constituted "a form of genocidal violence".[^cite_note-aljazeera20240709-156] Michael Fakhri, the United Nations Special Rapporteur on the Right to Food, stated that Israel is using famine as a tactic of genocide. Fakhri stated, "Israel made its intentions to starve everyone in Gaza explicit, implemented its plans and predictably created a famine throughout Gaza. Tracking the geography of Israel's starvation tactics alongside Israeli officials' statements confirms its intent."[^cite_note-468] In response to an Israeli bombing of four water engineers, [Oxfam](19698439.md) stated attacks on civilian infrastructure were "part of the crime of using starvation as a weapon of war."[^cite_note-469]

On 21 November 2024, the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) issued arrest warrants for Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and the former defence minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant). The judges wrote "The chamber considered that there are reasonable grounds to believe that both individuals intentionally and knowingly deprived the civilian population in Gaza of objects indispensable to their survival, including food, water, and medicine and medical supplies, as well as fuel and electricity" and that there are "reasonable grounds" that they bear criminal responsibility for "the war crime of starvation as a method of warfare".[^cite_note-470][^cite_note-ft20241121-3] The United States expressed deep concern over the "troubling process errors" that led to the decision to issue the warrants, adding that the US "fundamentally rejects" the decision.[^cite_note-El-Sabawi2024-471]

One of the key allegations in the [International Criminal Court arrest warrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_arrest_warrants_for_Israel-Hamas_war_figures) for Gallant and Netanyahu was the "use of starvation as a weapon of war".[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474][^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476] The use of starvation of civilians as weapon of war is banned by the United Nations.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478] In mid 2023 German government officials accused Russia of using hunger as a weapon but have not commented on Israel's policies as of March 2024.[^cite_note-ips_journal-479][^cite_note-ipg_journal-480] Gallant made a public speech in early October 2023, shortly after the [October 7 attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_7_attack) that sparked the war, saying, 'there will be no more electricity, no more food, no more fuel ... We are fighting against [human animals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_animals) and will behave accordingly'.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFabian2023-481][^cite_note-hrw20231218-69][^cite_note-bbc-51] A few weeks before that the arrest warrants were issued, there were also reports of looting occurring in areas controlled by the IDF.[^cite_note-482][^cite_note-483]

[Volker Türk](71721726.md), the UN high commissioner for human rights, stated that [Israel](9282173.md)'s restrictions on the entry of aid may constitute [starvation as a weapon of war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starvation_\(crime\)), which would be a [war crime](75113830.md).[^cite_note-484][^cite_note-485] An [Independent International Commission of Inquiry](69971286.md) also found Israel was using starvation as a method of war.[^cite_note-486] In April and May, USAID and the US State Department's Bureau of Population, Refugees and Migration determined that Israel was blocking food aid from entering Gaza. These findings were rejected by Secretary of State Blinken and the Biden Administration.[^cite_note-487]

### Israeli response
