The Israeli government denied that it was using starvation as a weapon of war and said that it was not violating the [Genocide Convention](143988.md).[^cite_note-488] [COGAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinator_of_Government_Activities_in_the_Territories), the Israeli agency responsible for allowing aid into Gaza, has stated Israel was not putting limits into the amount of aid entering Gaza.[^cite_note-489] COGAT's claim has been challenged by multiple entities, including the [European Union](9317.md), [United Nations](31769.md), [Oxfam](19698439.md), and [United Kingdom](31717.md).[^cite_note-490] Since March 2025, Israel has made the blockade publicly official,[^cite_note-491] with current defense minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) declaring "no humanitarian aid will enter Gaza".[^cite_note-492] Israel has claimed that "Hamas stockpiled supplies and kept them from increasingly desperate civilians,"[^cite_note-DeYoung_Hudson_2024_g056-104][^cite_note-493] but, as of February 2024, the US has not received evidence supporting this claim.[^cite_note-494] There have been reports of armed gangs stealing aid,[^cite_note-495] and some of those stealing aid have been armed by Israel.[^cite_note-496]

In August 2025, with the release of a video showing hostage [Evyatar David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidnapping_of_Evyatar_David) in emaciated condition many Israeli officials, released hostages and the Israeli public used it as a rally cry to show that Hamas was stealing humanitarian aid and to downplay the famine. This was due to the Hamas members arm shown in the video which appears to be slightly larger than David's with former hostages stating after the video was released that Hamas fighters guarding them daily had meat, vegetables, fish and fruit that they stole from humanitarian aid packages.[^cite_note-497][^cite_note-498]

## Israeli legal obligations

On 22 October 2025, the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) issued an [advisory opinion](14918.md#Advisory_opinions) that Israel is legally obliged to ensure that Gaza (and the rest of [Palestine](241405.md), as an [Israeli-occupied territory](2781576.md)) "has the essential supplies of daily life, including food, water, clothing, bedding, shelter, fuel, medical supplies and services"[^cite_note-ICJ_2025Oct22_obligations_IL-14]<sup>: 65 </sup> and that Israeli is obliged to "co-operate in good faith with the United Nations by providing every assistance ... including [UNRWA](32270.md)".[^cite_note-ICJ_2025Oct22_obligations_IL-14]<sup>: 66 </sup> Political scientist [Juan Cole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Cole) interpreted the advisory opinion as effectively declaring the Israeli food blockade illegal.[^cite_note-JuanCole_ICJ_IL_broke_law-499]

## Reactions

Israel's actions, according to its government, aim to neutralize Hamas as a security threat, including preventing military resources from being smuggled under the guise of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-500][^cite_note-cnn20231007-52] Efforts to address the crisis include attempts to negotiate [ceasefires](592591.md) and allow for the provision of aid, though challenges persist due to the continuation of hostilities and the difficulty in delivering aid amid the fighting.[^cite_note-washingtonpost20240304-272]

On 5 August 2024, the far-right Israeli Finance Minister Bezalel Smotrich stated that he believes that blocking humanitarian aid to the Gaza Strip is "justified and moral" even if it causes two million Gazans to die of starvation, but the international community would not allow it to happen.[^cite_note-timesofisrael20240805-501]

### January 2024

On 7 January, [Secretary-General of the United Nations](162415.md) [António Guterres](471806.md) stated "widespread famine looms" in Gaza.[^cite_note-502] The UN special rapporteur for health [Tlaleng Mofokeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlaleng_Mofokeng) responded to Guterres, stating Gaza was experiencing "deliberate starvation not famine".[^cite_note-503] Speaking at the United Nations Security Council on 12 January, Martin Griffiths stated colleagues who had made it into northern Gaza in recent days had described "scenes of utter horror: Corpses left lying in the road. People with evident signs of starvation stopping trucks in search of anything they can get to survive."[^cite_note-504]

On 11 January, the Israeli [Coordinator of Government Activities in the Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinator_of_Government_Activities_in_the_Territories) stated there was no hunger in Gaza.[^cite_note-505] On 16 January, Michael Fakhri, the [UN special rapporteur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_special_rapporteur) on food, stated, "What we're witnessing in Gaza is an entire civilian population made to go hungry... this is a result of Israeli bombardment, this is a result of the denial of humanitarian relief. We've never seen anything so brutal happen so quickly".[^cite_note-506] Following a visit to the [Rafah border crossing](10475618.md), US Senator Chris Van Hollen described the entry of humanitarian aid into Gaza as an "unnecessarily cumbersome process".[^cite_note-507]

The heads of the WFP, UNICEF, and the World Health Organization issued a joint statement stating significantly more humanitarian aid was needed in Gaza.[^cite_note-508] [Mohammad Mustafa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Mustafa_\(politician\)), the chief economist of the [Palestine Investment Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Investment_Fund), stated, "Maybe more people will be killed or die from hunger and famine than the war itself."[^cite_note-509] After major Western donors announced they were suspending funding of UNRWA, the agency stated "over 2 million people \[are] depending on it for their sheer survival" as "hunger stalks everyone."[^cite_note-510]

### February 2024

On 14 February, a joint statement by 14 major human rights organizations, including [Action Against Hunger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_Against_Hunger), ActionAid, [Danish Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_Refugee_Council), [Handicap International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handicap_International), [INTERSOS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INTERSOS), [Islamic Relief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Relief), Mercy Corps, Norwegian Refugee Council, Plan International, [Project HOPE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_HOPE), Save the Children, [Solidarités International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarit%C3%A9s_International), and [War Child UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_Child_\(charity\)) stated, "The risk of famine is increasing each day in Gaza due to the continuation of hostilities, and the continued blockade of the Strip."[^cite_note-511]

The [American Friends Service Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Friends_Service_Committee) stated, "Everyone is hungry in Gaza today. That is just enormous and truly catastrophic, and we've never seen anything like that before."[^cite_note-512] Alex de Waal, a British academic, stated, "There's no doubt that certain senior members of the Israel government and certain groups within Israeli society have the intent of starving Gaza."[^cite_note-513] He further stated, "Nothing compares to Gaza over the last 75 years."[^cite_note-514]

On 18 February, the heads of eight major humanitarian organizations—including the Norwegian Refugee Council, Mercy Corps, Refugees International, [Oxfam America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxfam_America), [CARE USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CARE_USA), Save the Children, Action Against Hunger, and [Catholic Relief Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Relief_Services)—wrote a joint op-ed, stating, "If the situation continues we will see one of the biggest disasters we have faced as humanitarians... this crisis will soon reach a tipping point, where emergency food aid won't be enough. Averting mass death becomes harder as starvation gains momentum."[^cite_note-515] The Gaza Media Office stated on 20 February, "We hold the US administration and the international community additionally to Israel fully responsible for this famine."[^cite_note-516]

On 21 February, WFP chief Cindy McCain stated, "A famine doesn't have to happen. But if things don't change, it will".[^cite_note-517] [Tor Wennesland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tor_Wennesland) stated on 22 February that more than 2 million people were facing extreme food insecurity.[^cite_note-518] On 23 February, a UNOCHA official stated, "Famine is looming".[^cite_note-519] The head of the [Red Cross](9720597.md) stated on 26 February: "80% of the population already faces emergency or catastrophic acute food insecurity conditions".[^cite_note-520] Ramesh Rajasingham, the UN humanitarian affairs chief, stated that if nothing was done to change the status quo then "widespread famine in Gaza is almost inevitable".[^cite_note-521] On 27 February, Michael Fakhri, the UN Special Rapporteur on the Right to Food, said that Gaza was going through a genocide and that there was "no reason to intentionally block the passage of humanitarian aid or intentionally obliterate small-scale fishing vessels, greenhouses and orchards in Gaza – other than to deny people access to food."[^cite_note-guardian_2.28.24-522]

On 29 February, the humanitarian policy director of Oxfam America stated the organization was opposed to proposed US airdrops, stating, "Oxfam does not support US airdrops to Gaza, which would mostly serve to relieve the guilty consciences of senior US officials whose policies are contributing to the ongoing atrocities and risk of famine in Gaza".[^cite_note-523]

The UN WFP warned of a real prospect of famine by May 2024, for half a million people.[^cite_note-famine-119]

### March 2024

A man in Rafah spoke to Al Jazeera, stating, "From the moment we wake up until the moment we sleep, we are battling to survive. We are fighting to get our hands on some water, to get hold of a loaf of bread for our children. We are exhausted, mentally and physically. This is unbearable."[^cite_note-524] Save the Children released a statement saying children in Gaza were "forced to forage for scraps of food left by rats and eating leaves out of desperation. The risk of famine will increase so long as the government of Israel continues to impede the entry of aid."[^cite_note-525] UNICEF responded to news of children dying from starvation, stating, "Now, the child deaths we feared are here and are likely to rapidly increase unless the war ends and obstacles to humanitarian relief are immediately resolved"[^cite_note-526] On 5 March, UNOCHA stated that the first deaths of children from starvation in northern Gaza were "a warning like no other".[^cite_note-527] A WFP director stated, "The is nowhere else in the world where so many people face imminent famine".[^cite_note-528]

In *The Guardian*, global health expert [Devi Sridhar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devi_Sridhar) wrote there was no precedent to the starvation in Gaza. Sridhar quoted an unnamed colleague, who stated there had never been another conflict in history "that used bombing, snipers, starvation all at once with such intensity".[^cite_note-529] On 6 March, a UN official stated, "Ninety percent of the population today in Gaza is facing a high level of acute food insecurity".[^cite_note-530] A medical student working as a doctor at [Al-Shifa Hospital](21218880.md) documented the first death by starvation in Gaza; he stated, "Do not talk to us again about human rights, I do not know where the world has reached in its brutality and cruelty."[^cite_note-531] Gaza's civil defense criticized humanitarian aid drops, stating, "The method of using the dropping of aid via international relief planes has not limited the famine crisis".[^cite_note-532] The EU's humanitarian aid chief [Janez Lenarcic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janez_Lenarcic) stated, "We already have a very strong and credible indication that there are pockets of famine already in the Gaza Strip."[^cite_note-533]

A displaced Palestinian speaking to [Al Jazeera English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English) in late March stated, "The situation is so bad that no one can imagine it".[^cite_note-534] Martin Griffiths stated the world should "hang its head in shame for failing to stop this".[^cite_note-535] In a report, Islamic Relief stated, "Palestinians are not just starving; they are being starved".[^cite_note-536] The [International Rescue Committee](196452.md), stated, "Children are starving due to an entirely man-made and preventable crisis. There is no excuse".[^cite_note-537] Melanie Ward, the CEO of Medical Aid for Palestinians, stated, "If Israel would only let food aid in, we could stop the starvation immediately."[^cite_note-538] Oxfam stated the imminent famine was the result of Israel's bombing of Gaza and "using starvation as a weapon of war".[^cite_note-539]

Citing the IPC, the US Secretary of State acknowledged on 19 March: "100% of the population in Gaza is at severe levels of acute food insecurity. That's the first time an entire population has been so classified".[^cite_note-540] [Naledi Pandor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naledi_Pandor), South Africa's Foreign Minister, stated that as famine set in, humanity needed to look at itself "in horror and dismay".[^cite_note-541] Caroline Gennez, the Belgian Minister of Development, stated Israel would not defeat Hamas by using hunger as a weapon to starve civilians.[^cite_note-542] The [World Bank](36752.md) stated the "situation in the Gaza Strip has reached catastrophic levels".[^cite_note-543] [Rishi Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak), the prime minister of the United Kingdom, stated under questioning: "We need urgent action now to avoid a famine".[^cite_note-544] On 18 March 2024, the UN secretary-general António Guterres stated the imminent famine was an "entirely man-made disaster".[^cite_note-un20240318-9]

After the passage of [UNSC Resolution 2728](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2728), the UN director of Human Rights Watch stated Israel needed to begin "facilitating the delivery of humanitarian aid, ending its starvation of Gaza's population, and halting unlawful attacks".[^cite_note-545] A provisional measure issued by the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) (ICJ) in *[South Africa v. Israel](75736183.md)* found "Palestinians in Gaza are no longer facing only a risk of famine... but that famine is setting in".[^cite_note-reuters20240328-133] David J. Simon, the Director of the Genocide Studies Program at [Yale University](34273.md), wrote, "Failing to act raises the possibility the \[ICJ] will make a finding of genocide".[^cite_note-546] Amnesty International responded to the ICJ's provisional measures, stating, "This new ruling must serve as a critical reminder to all states of their clear duty to prevent genocide".[^cite_note-547]

### April 2024

A director of Save the Children stated, "Starvation must never be used as a weapon of war—27 children have already been killed by starvation and disease. If the world fails to act now countless more children will be added to that number."[^cite_note-548] On 16 April, Andrea De Domenico, the head of UNOCHA in Palestine, stated, "We're dealing with this dance where we do one step forward, two steps backward, or two steps forward, one step backward, which leaves us basically always at the same point".[^cite_note-549] On 23 April, [David M. Satterfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_M._Satterfield), the US special envoy for humanitarian issues, stated that the risk of famine throughout Gaza, and especially in the north, was "very high".[^cite_note-550] Gian Caro Cirri, a WFP director, stated, "There is reasonable evidence that all three famine thresholds – food insecurity, malnutrition and mortality—will be passed in the next six weeks."[^cite_note-551] Carl Skau, the World Food Program deputy executive director, stated, "We are still heading toward famine. We haven't seen the paradigm shift that is needed to avert a famine".[^cite_note-552]

### May 2024

On 4 May, Cindy McCain, the head of the WFP, stated in an interview with [NBC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC_News) that Northern Gaza was in a "full-blown famine", and that it was "moving its way south". She said: "It's horror. It's so hard to look at and it's so hard to hear. What we are asking for and what we continually ask for is a ceasefire and the ability to have unfettered access, to get in safe through the various ports and gate crossings."[^cite_note-553] A WFP spokesman later said that one of the three benchmarks for a formal famine declaration had been met.[^cite_note-554] Human Rights Watch stated, "Despite children dying from starvation and famine in Gaza, the Israeli authorities are still blocking aid critical for the survival of Gaza's population in defiance of the World Court".[^cite_note-555] The American Friends Service Committee stated, "There are 600,000 children on the edge of death."[^cite_note-556]

### June 2024

Stacy Gilbert, a [US State Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_State_Department) official who resigned in protest of the Biden's Administration's policies, stated it was "absolutely unanimous that Israel is blocking humanitarian assistance, which is why there's famine".[^cite_note-557] The Middle East and North Africa director of Oxfam stated, "When hunger claims many more lives, nobody will be able to deny the horrifying impact of Israel's deliberate, illegal and cruel obstruction of aid".[^cite_note-558] In an interview, the head of the Kamal Adwan Hospital stated, "We are reeling under famine, we are left with nothing but some quantities of white flour."[^cite_note-559]

Israel has criticized the IPC's previous methodology,[^cite_note-timesofisrael20240531-153] citing the revised June FRC report which stated that: "In contrast with the assumptions made for the projection period (March–July 2024), the amount of food and non-food commodities allowed into the northern governorates increased"; "Additionally, the response in the nutrition, water sanitation and hygiene (WASH) and health sectors was scaled up. In this context, the available evidence does not indicate that famine is currently occurring."[^cite_note-560] A separate peer-reviewed study by Israeli public health academics analyzed data from all food shipments delivered into the Gaza Strip by land between January–July 2024 by COGAT, including the gross weight of each consignment and its content, concluded that the provisions entering Gaza were sufficient to provide the basic requirements of energy, protein and fat for the entire population.[^cite_note-561] An independent study by researchers from [Columbia university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_Business_School) came to similar conclusions, though has not been peer-reviewed.[^cite_note-562][^cite_note-563]

Arif Husain, the chief economist of WFP, stated that arguments about an official famine designation for Gaza were missing the point, as the situation was already dire for the territory's 2.3 million people.[^cite_note-564]

### July 2024 – present

In July 2024, US Vice President [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) stated in an interview that she was aware of reports that people were eating grass, as well as Gaza's lack of available clean water, stating, "You can't make shit with flour if you don't have clean water. So what's going on with that? I ask questions like, What are people actually eating right now?"[^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566]

In August 2024, [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md), the Israeli finance minister, stated, "We bring in aid because there is no choice... We can't, in the current global reality, manage a war. Nobody will let us cause 2 million civilians to die of hunger, even though it might be justified and moral".[^cite_note-timesofisrael20240805-501] The European Union and foreign ministries of France and the UK condemned Smotrich's comments.[^cite_note-567] Australian foreign minister [Penny Wong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penny_Wong) stated, "The deliberate starvation of civilians is a war crime. There is no justification for it, ever."[^cite_note-568] In October 2024, Oxfam warned that the effects of starvation were likely to last for generations.[^cite_note-569]

On 2 October 2024, 99 American healthcare workers who have served in the Gaza Strip since 7 October 2023, sent US President [Joe Biden](145422.md), US Vice President [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris), and others a letter, cited in a paper from the [Watson Institute for International and Public Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watson_Institute_for_International_and_Public_Affairs) at [Brown University](4157.md), in which they said that based on starvation standards by the US-funded [Integrated Food Security Phase Classification](16431152.md), according to the most conservative estimate that they could calculate based on the available data, at least 62,413 people in Gaza were likely to have died from starvation, most of them young children.[^cite_note-MotherJonesTotalDeaths-570][^cite_note-WatsonInstituteEstimatedDeaths-571][^cite_note-GazaAppendix-572]

In April 2025, Israeli Defense Minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) voiced support for the blockade of humanitarian aid to Gaza, stating, "Israel's policy is clear: no humanitarian aid will enter Gaza, and blocking this aid is one of the main pressure levers preventing Hamas from using it as a tool with the population."[^cite_note-573]

On 16 May 2025, during the last day of his [visit to the Gulf Arab states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_2025_visit_by_Donald_Trump_to_the_Middle_East), President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) said, "We're looking at Gaza. And we're going to get that taken care of. A lot of people are starving" while in Abu Dhabi.[^cite_note-TheGuardian16May2025-574][^cite_note-CBSNews16May2025-575] In May 2025, the United Kingdom, France, and Canada threatened [sanctions against Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sanctions_involving_Israel) over Israel's blockade of humanitarian aid to the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-576]

On 25 July 2025, US Senator [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) said in a statement: "Having already killed or wounded 200,000 Palestinians, mostly women and children, the extremist Israeli government is using mass starvation to engineer the ethnic cleansing of Gaza."[^cite_note-577] On 26 July 2025, House Democratic Leader [Hakeem Jeffries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakeem_Jeffries) called on the [Trump administration](78298108.md) to pressure Israel to end the blockade, saying, "The starvation and death of Palestinian children and civilians in an ongoing war zone is unacceptable. The Trump administration has the ability to bring an end to this humanitarian crisis. They must act now."[^cite_note-578] In July 2025, [World Health Organization](33583.md) Director-General [Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus](39712205.md) said, "I don't know what you'd call it other than mass starvation. And it is man-made."[^cite_note-579]

On 27 July 2025, Israeli Prime Minister Netanyahu claimed that there is "no starvation in Gaza, no policy of starvation in Gaza."[^cite_note-580][^cite_note-581] Netanyahu's statement was contradicted by US President Trump, who admitted that people in Gaza were starving and declared that the US and its allies would establish barrier-free food centers in Gaza to assist with humanitarian aid deliveries.[^cite_note-582]

On 30 July, resolutions proposed by Sanders that would block weapons to Israel failed, though were supported by the majority of the [Senate Democratic Caucus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Democratic_Caucus); several moderate Democrats who had previously opposed blocking weapons voted for the resolutions over the increased level of starvation.[^cite_note-583][^cite_note-584]

Irish Prime Minister [Micheál Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miche%C3%A1l_Martin),[^cite_note-585] Polish Prime Minister [Donald Tusk](648724.md),[^cite_note-586] British Prime Minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md), French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) and German Chancellor [Friedrich Merz](1967626.md) accused Israel of starving the people of Gaza and urged an end to the blockade.[^cite_note-587]

In August 2025, German newspapers *[Bild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bild)* and *[Süddeutsche Zeitung](30873120.md)* published articles questioning the authenticity of photos from the Gaza Strip, with *Bild* accusing Anas Zayed Fteiha, a [photojournalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Photojournalist) working for Turkey's [Anadolu Agency](43356543.md), of staging his photos of the famine. The articles were cited by Israel's [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](18133296.md) as evidence of "[Pallywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallywood)".[^cite_note-588] Fteiha denied the accusations and accused *Bild* of repeated breaches of [journalistic ethics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Journalistic_ethics). He stated, "The photo they published to distort me is entirely real. It was taken during the filming of a documentary documenting the famine in Gaza, as children were scrambling for food or water. Everything in it was real, not staged or directed."[^cite_note-589][^cite_note-ArabNews080825-590] The claims made in the *Bild* report were debunked by the Israeli [fact-checking](595273.md) organization [FakeReporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FakeReporter) and the French newspaper *[Libération](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lib%C3%A9ration)*.[^cite_note-591][^cite_note-ArabNews080825-590]

In response to the increasing number of deaths due to malnutrition, and heightened reporting, the IDF issued a statement on 12 August blaming the deaths on a deliberate campaign by Hamas to interfere with ceasefire negotiations. They also claimed reports obfuscated [preexisting conditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complication_\(medicine\)). Experts interviewed by *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)* on 13 August stated that such conditions do not preclude the cause of the deaths being due to starvation, and the existing conditions in Gaza can aggravate preexisting conditions.[^cite_note-592] In August 2025, [The Free Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Free_Press_\(CBS_News\)) investigated the pre-existing health problems of starving Palestinian children in an attempt to cast doubt on the famine.[^cite_note-593]

In August 2025, [Xaviaer DuRousseau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xaviaer_DuRousseau) and other American and Israeli social media influencers went on a paid trip to Gaza by the [diaspora affairs ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Diaspora_Affairs_and_Combating_Antisemitism) to film and share content from the distribution sites. According to *Haaretz*, the aim of the campaign is to "reveal the truth" about the conditions in Gaza.[^cite_note-594] DuRousseau stated on [Twitter](9988187.md), "There is enough food at this aid base to feed every person in Gaza for at least a week, but the UN, Hamas, etc refuse to distribute the food efficiently. Instead, it sits here to spoil and be stolen. How's that Israel's fault?".[^cite_note-595]

In September 2025, *[Drop Site News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drop_Site_News)* reported that [Google](1092923.md) had signed a six-month, \$45 million contract with the Israeli government in June to push its [propaganda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasbara) during the [Gaza war](74998519.md), including content that denied the famine.[^cite_note-Poulson-2025-596][^cite_note-597]

[^cite_note-2]: &#x20;The total number of deaths from malnutrition and starvation reported by the Gaza Health Ministry was 440 as of 10 September, including 147 children.<sup>[\[1\]](#cite_note-1)</sup>

[^cite_note-11]: &#x20;According to [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem), the famine risk is a direct outcome of Israeli policy: "This reality is not a byproduct of war, but a direct result of Israel's declared policy. Residents now depend entirely on food supplies from outside Gaza, as they can no longer produce almost any food themselves. Most cultivated fields have been destroyed, and accessing open areas during the war is dangerous in any case. Bakeries, factories and food warehouses have been bombed or shut down due to lack of basic supplies, fuel and electricity."<sup>[\[9\]](#cite_note-Israel_is_starving-10)</sup>

[^cite_note-37]: &#x20;The complicated procedures for obtaining clearances from Israel at the transit points also caused notable spoilage further reducing the food allowed in. Prospective import goods had to arrive in Israeli trucks, which were unloaded as the goods were transferred to 'neutral' trucks that then were allowed transit to the Gaza side. Once there, the consignments had to be unloaded from the neutral trucks and reloaded on Gazan trucks.<sup>[\[34\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward-Hassmann2016127-36)</sup>

[^cite_note-41]: &#x20;The agricultural sector, including crop farming, fisheries, livestock farming and poultry farming, suffered direct losses worth some US\$170 million. Indirect losses have still to be definitively calculated. One business organization estimates that 60 per cent of all agricultural land had been destroyed, 40 per cent directly during the military operations. Moreover, 17 per cent of all orchards, 8.3 per cent of livestock, 2.6 per cent of poultry, 18.1 per cent of hatcheries, 25.6 per cent of beehives, 9.2 per cent of open fields and 13 per cent of groundwater wells were destroyed. Agriculture had already lost a third of its capacity since the start of the second intifada and the frequent Israeli incursions, according to NGO estimates used by UNDP-Gaza ... Some 250 agricultural wells were reportedly destroyed or severely damaged.<sup>[\[34\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward-Hassmann2016127-36)</sup><sup>[\[37\]](#cite_note-40)</sup>

This ration included 140 grams of flour, 70 grams of rice and of potatoes, 232 grams of meat, 279 grams of vegetables, and 40 grams of legumes. Together with allocations for fruit, milk, oil and sugar, the total amounted to 1,886 grams per person per day, significantly less than the average daily food consumption by Israelis. These estimates were used by the Ministry of Defence to calculate the 'Daily humanitarian portion required to enter Gaza Strip', which were applied until 2010."

[^cite_note-MotherJonesTotalDeaths-570]: &#x20;Hurwitz, Sophie (8 October 2024). ["Report: In one year, more than 100,000 deaths in Gaza—aided by over \$17.9 billion from the US"](https://www.motherjones.com/politics/2024/10/us-israel-funding-gaza-palestine-deaths-october-100000-17-billion/). *Mother Jones*. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20250629012044/https://www.motherjones.com/politics/2024/10/us-israel-funding-gaza-palestine-deaths-october-100000-17-billion/) from the original on 29 June 2025. Retrieved 9 August 2025.

[^cite_note-WatsonInstituteEstimatedDeaths-571]: &#x20;Stamatopoulou-Robbins, Sophia (7 October 2024). ["The Human Toll: Indirect Deaths from War in Gaza and the West Bank, October 7, 2023 Forward"](https://watson.brown.edu/costsofwar/files/cow/imce/papers/2023/2024/Costs%20of%20War_Human%20Toll%20Since%20Oct%207.pdf) (PDF). [Watson Institute for International and Public Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watson_Institute_for_International_and_Public_Affairs), [Brown University](4157.md). [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20250710072600/https://watson.brown.edu/costsofwar/files/cow/imce/papers/2023/2024/Costs%20of%20War_Human%20Toll%20Since%20Oct%207.pdf) (PDF) from the original on 10 July 2025. Retrieved 17 October 2024. In addition to killing people directly through traumatic injuries, wars cause "indirect deaths" by destroying, damaging, or causing deterioration of economic, social, psychological and health conditions. Most expansively, this report describes the causal pathways that can be expected to lead to far larger numbers of indirect deaths. These deaths result from diseases and other population-level health effects that stem from war's destruction of public infrastructure and livelihood sources, reduced access to water and sanitation, environmental damage, and other such factors. This report builds on a foundation of previous Costs of War research for its framework and methodology in covering the most significant chains of impact, or causal pathways, to indirect war deaths in Gaza and the West Bank. Unlike in combat, these deaths do not necessarily occur immediately or in the close aftermath of the battles which many observers focus on. While it will take years to assess the full extent of these population-level health effects, they will inevitably lead to far higher numbers of deaths than direct violence.
